\displaystyle+\underbrace{\delta^{3}\sum_{n}\frac{\eta}{1+\eta\lambda_{n}^{(3)}}\Big\langle\tilde{\mathbb{L}},\widetilde{\textit{err}}_{1,\textit{al}}(e^{(3)}_{n})\Big\rangle\otimes\int_{B_{i}}e^{(3)}_{n}(\xi)d\xi}_{:=\textit{err}_{1,i}}+\underbrace{\delta^{3}\sum_{n}\frac{\eta}{1+\eta\lambda_{n}^{(3)}}\Big\langle\tilde{\mathbb{L}},\widetilde{\textit{err}}_{2,\textit{al}}(e^{(3)}_{n})\Big\rangle\otimes\int_{B_{i}}e^{(3)}_{n}(\xi)d\xi}_{:=\textit{err}_{2,i}},

(3.33)

where we recall that

\displaystyle\widetilde{\textit{err}}_{1,\textit{al}}\big[e_{n}^{(3)}\big]

\displaystyle=\frac{\omega^{2}}{2}\delta^{2}\mathbb{N}_{B_{i}}^{(\mathrm{0})}\big[e_{n}^{(3)}\big](\mathrm{x})+\frac{i\omega^{3}\beta^{2}_{\mathrm{m}}}{12\pi}\delta^{3}\int_{B_{i}}e_{n}^{(3)}(\xi)d\xi-\frac{\omega^{2}}{2}\delta^{2}\int_{B_{i}}\mathbb{G}^{(\mathrm{0})}(\varsigma,\xi)\frac{\mathrm{A}(\varsigma,\xi)\cdot e_{n}^{(3)}}{\|\varsigma-\xi\|^{2}}d\xi

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4\pi}\sum_{\mathrm{j}\geq 3}\frac{(i\omega\beta^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\mathrm{m}})^{\mathrm{j}+1}}{(\mathrm{j}+1)!}\delta^{j+1}\int_{B_{i}}\underset{\varsigma}{\textit{Hess}}(\|\varsigma-\xi\|^{\mathrm{j}})\cdot e_{n}^{(3)}(\xi)d\xi,

(3.34)

and

\displaystyle\widetilde{\textit{err}}_{2,\textit{al}}\big[e_{n}^{(3)}\big]=\delta^{2}\mathbb{N}_{B_{i}}^{(\mathrm{0})}\big[e_{n}^{(3)}\big](\mathrm{x})+\frac{i\omega\beta^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\mathrm{m}}}{4\pi}\delta^{3}\int_{B_{i}}e_{n}^{(3)}(\xi)d\xi-\frac{1}{4\pi}\sum_{\mathrm{j}\geq 1}\frac{(i\omega\beta^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\mathrm{m}})^{\mathrm{j}+1}}{(\mathrm{j}+1)!}\delta^{j+1}\int_{B_{i}}\|\varsigma-\xi\|^{\mathrm{j}}\ e_{n}^{(3)}(\xi)d\xi.

(3.35)

It can be observed in both expressions for
\widetilde{\textit{err}}_{1,\textit{al}}\big[e_{n}^{(3)}\big]
or
\widetilde{\textit{err}}_{2,\textit{al}}\big[e_{n}^{(3)}\big]
, the presence of factors such as
\delta^{2}
and
\delta^{3}
, hence, we see that the dominating term is
\delta^{2}\mathbb{N}_{B_{i}}^{(\mathrm{0})}\big[e_{n}^{(3)}\big].
We begin by analyzing the term
\textit{err}_{1,i}
:

\displaystyle|\textit{err}_{1,i}|

\displaystyle:=\delta^{3}\Big|\sum_{n}\frac{\eta}{1+\eta\lambda_{n}^{(3)}}\Big\langle\tilde{\mathbb{L}},\widetilde{\textit{err}}_{1,\textit{al}}(e^{(3)}_{n})\Big\rangle\otimes\int_{B_{i}}e^{(3)}_{n}(\xi)d\xi\Big|

\displaystyle\simeq\delta^{5}\Big|\sum_{n}\frac{\eta}{1+\eta\lambda_{n}^{(3)}}\Big\langle\tilde{\mathbb{L}},\mathbb{N}_{B_{i}}^{(\mathrm{0})}\big[e_{n}^{(3)}\big]\Big\rangle\otimes\int_{B_{i}}e^{(3)}_{n}(\xi)d\xi\Big|+\delta^{6}\Big|\sum_{n}\frac{\eta}{1+\eta\lambda_{n}^{(3)}}\Big\langle\tilde{\mathbb{L}},\int_{B_{i}}e_{n}^{(3)}(\xi)d\xi\Big\rangle\otimes\int_{B_{i}}e^{(3)}_{n}(\xi)d\xi\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta^{5-h}\|\tilde{\mathbb{L}}\|_{L^{2}(B_{i})},

A similar estimate holds for the term
\textit{err}_{2,i}
. Based on these previous estimates for
\textit{err}_{1,i}
and
\textit{err}_{2,i}
, we can conclude

\displaystyle\mathbcal{P}_{D_{i}}

\displaystyle=\delta^{3}\sum_{n}\frac{1}{1+\eta\lambda_{n}^{(3)}}\int_{B_{i}}e^{(3)}_{n}(\xi)d\xi\otimes\int_{B_{i}}e^{(3)}_{n}(\xi)d\xi+\mathcal{O}\Big(\delta^{5-h}\|\tilde{\mathbb{L}}\|_{L^{2}(B_{i})}\Big).

(3.36)

Next, we estimate the term
\|\tilde{\mathbb{L}}\|_{L^{2}(B_{i})}
:

\displaystyle\|\tilde{\mathbb{L}}\|_{L^{2}(B_{i})}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{n}\big|\langle\tilde{\mathbb{L}},e_{n}^{(1)}\rangle\big|^{2}+\sum_{n}\big|\langle\tilde{\mathbb{L}},e_{n}^{(3)}\rangle\big|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{n}\frac{1}{|1+\eta\lambda_{n}^{(3)}|^{2}}|\langle\mathbb{I},e_{n}^{(3)}\rangle|^{2}+\sum_{n}\frac{|\eta|^{2}}{|1+\eta\lambda_{n}^{(3)}|^{2}}\Big|\langle\tilde{\mathbb{L}},\widetilde{\textit{err}}_{1,\textit{al}}(e^{(3)}_{n})\rangle\Big|^{2}+\sum_{n}\frac{|\eta|^{2}}{|1+\eta\lambda_{n}^{(3)}|^{2}}\Big|\langle\tilde{\mathbb{L}},\widetilde{\textit{err}}_{2,\textit{al}}(e^{(3)}_{n})\rangle\Big|^{2}.

Thus, we obtain

\displaystyle\|\tilde{\mathbb{L}}\|_{L^{2}(B_{i})}^{2}\lesssim\mathcal{O}(\delta^{-2h})+\mathcal{O}(\delta^{4-2h}\|\tilde{\mathbb{L}}\|_{L^{2}(B_{i})}^{2}).

(3.37)

By choosing,

h<2

we can further deduce that

\displaystyle\|\tilde{\mathbb{L}}\|_{L^{2}(B_{i})}\lesssim\delta^{-h}.

(3.38)

Consequently, using the above estimate and plugging it in (
3.36
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbcal{P}_{D_{i}}=\delta^{3}\sum_{n}\frac{1}{1+\eta\lambda_{n}^{(3)}}\int_{B_{i}}e^{(3)}_{n}(\xi)d\xi\otimes\int_{B_{i}}e^{(3)}_{n}(\xi)d\xi+\mathcal{O}\big(\delta^{5-2h}\big).

(3.39)

Again, substituting the estimate for
\|\tilde{\mathbb{L}}\|_{L^{2}(B_{i})}
into equation
(\ref{first-al-1})
, we derive

\displaystyle\int_{D_{i}}\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}(E_{i})(y)\ dy

\displaystyle-\eta\sum_{j\neq i}^{M}\mathbcal{P}_{D_{i}}\cdot\bm{\Upsilon}^{(k)}(z_{i},z_{j})\int_{D_{j}}\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}(E_{j})(y)dy=\mathbcal{P}_{D_{i}}\cdot\mathrm{E}^{\textit{in}}(z_{i})+\mathcal{O}\Big(\delta^{4-h}\Big)

\displaystyle+\mathcal{O}\Big(\delta^{\frac{11}{2}-h}d^{-4}\underset{j}{\max}\|\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}(E_{j})\|_{L^{2}(D_{j})}\Big)+\mathcal{O}\Big(\delta^{\frac{11}{2}-h}d^{-3}\underset{j}{\max}\|\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}(E_{j})\|_{L^{2}(D_{j})}\Big)

\displaystyle+\mathcal{O}\Big(\delta^{\frac{13}{2}-h}|\log(d)|d^{-3}\underset{j}{\max}\|\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}(E_{j})\|_{L^{2}(D_{j})}\Big).

(3.40)

The above algebraic system is invertible under the following condition, as discussed in
[
8
, Section 6.1]
:

\displaystyle\eta\underset{j=1,2,\ldots,M}{\max}\|\mathbcal{P}_{D_{i}}\|_{L^{\infty}(\bm{\Omega})}d^{-3}<1.

To finalize the proof of Lemma
3.3
, we require the following proposition, which states that:

3.2.2
A-Priori Estimates in
\mathbb{L}^{4}(D)

Proof of Proposition
2.2
.

Consider the electromagnetic scattering problem (
1.2
) for cluster of plasmonic nanoparticles
D_{i}
for
i=1,2,\ldots,M.
Then, for
h,\lambda
satisfying

\displaystyle 3-3\lambda-h\geq 0\quad\text{and}\quad\frac{9}{5}<h<2,

we have the following a-priori estimates

\displaystyle\underset{i}{\max}\big\|\mathrm{E}_{i}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(D_{i})}\lesssim\delta^{\frac{3}{2}-h}.

In addition to that, we have

\displaystyle\big\|E_{i}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{4}}(D_{i})}\sim\delta^{\frac{3}{4}-\mathrm{h}}.

Proof.

The proof of the Proposition is structured into two parts. We begin by deriving an a priori estimate in the space
\mathbb{L}^{2}(D)
.

Part 1: Derivation of the
\mathbb{L}^{2}(D)
Estimate

We derive the proof by projecting the Lippmann-Schwinger equation (
3.17
) onto each of the three sub-spaces outlined in (
1.12
).

1. Projection onto
\mathbb{H}_{0}(\textit{div}\ 0,D_{i})
.

We first consider the Lippmann-Schwinger equation (
3.17
)

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}_{i}(\mathrm{x})+\eta\;\mathbb{M}_{D_{i}}^{(k)}\big[\mathrm{E}_{i}\big](\mathrm{x})=\mathrm{E}^{\textit{in}}(\mathrm{x})-\eta\sum_{j\neq m}^{M}\Big(\mathbb{M}_{D_{j}}^{(\kappa)}\big[\mathrm{E}_{j}\big](\mathrm{x})-k^{2}\mathbb{N}_{D_{j}}^{(\kappa)}\Big)\big[\mathrm{E}_{i}\big](\mathrm{x})+k^{2}\eta\;\mathbb{N}_{D_{i}}^{(\kappa)}\big[\mathrm{E}_{i}\big](\mathrm{x})

(3.41)

Then, we note that
\mathbb{M}^{(\kappa)}[\mathrm{E}_{i}]
vanishes in the subspace
\mathbb{H}_{0}(\textit{div}\ 0,D_{i})
. Consequently, projecting the above equation onto
\mathbb{H}_{0}(\textit{div}\ 0,D_{i})
yields the following equation for
x\in D_{i}

\displaystyle\big\langle\mathrm{E}_{i},e_{n}^{(1)}\big\rangle=\big\langle\mathrm{E}^{\textit{in}},e_{n}^{(1)}\big\rangle+k^{2}\eta\big\langle\mathbb{N}^{(\kappa)}_{D_{i}}\big[\mathrm{E}_{i}\big](\mathrm{x}),e_{n}^{(1)}\big\rangle+k^{2}\eta\sum_{j\neq i}^{M}\big\langle\mathbb{N}^{(\kappa)}_{D_{j}}\big[\mathrm{E}_{j}\big](\mathrm{x}),e_{n}^{(1)}\big\rangle,

which simplifies to

\displaystyle\big\langle\mathrm{E}_{i},e_{n}^{(1)}\big\rangle=\big\langle\mathrm{E}^{\textit{in}},e_{n}^{(1)}\big\rangle+k^{2}\eta\big\langle\mathbb{N}^{(\kappa)}_{D_{i}}\big[\mathrm{E}_{i}\big](\mathrm{x}),e_{n}^{(1)}\big\rangle+k^{2}\eta\ \textit{Err}^{(1)}_{n,D_{j}},

(3.42)

where we define the error term as after expanding it using Taylor’s series expansion

\displaystyle\textit{Err}^{(1)}_{n,D_{j}}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{j\neq i}^{M}\Big\langle\mathbb{G}^{(k)}(z_{i},z_{j})\int_{D_{j}}\mathrm{E}_{j}(y)dy;e_{n}^{(1)}\Big\rangle

\displaystyle+\sum_{j\neq i}^{M}\Big\langle\int_{0}^{1}\underset{x}{\nabla}\mathbb{G}^{(k)}(z_{i}+\theta(x-z_{i}),z_{j})\cdot(x-z_{i})d\theta\int_{D_{j}}\mathrm{E}_{j}(y)dy;e_{n}^{(1)}\Big\rangle

\displaystyle+\sum_{j\neq i}^{M}\Big\langle\int_{D_{j}}\int_{0}^{1}\underset{y}{\nabla}\mathbb{G}^{(k)}(z_{i},z_{j}+\theta(y-z_{j}))\cdot(y-z_{j})\mathrm{E}_{j}(y)d\theta dy;e_{n}^{(1)}\Big\rangle.

(3.43)

Upon re-scaling to the domains
B_{i}
and
B_{j}
in equation (
3.42
), we obtain

\displaystyle\big\langle\tilde{\mathrm{E}}_{i},\tilde{e}_{n}^{(1)}\big\rangle

\displaystyle=\big\langle\tilde{\mathrm{E}}^{\textit{in}},\tilde{e}_{n}^{(1)}\big\rangle+k^{2}\eta\ \delta^{2}\big\langle\mathbb{N}_{B_{i}}^{(\kappa)}\big[\tilde{\mathrm{E}}_{i}\big](\mathrm{x}),\tilde{e}_{n}^{(1)}\big\rangle+k^{2}\eta\ \widetilde{\textit{Err}}^{(1)}_{n,B_{j}}.

Taking the squared modulus and summing over
n
, we derive

\displaystyle\|\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}(\tilde{\mathrm{E}}_{i})\|^{2}_{L^{2}(B_{i})}\lesssim\|\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}(\tilde{\mathrm{E}}^{\textit{in}})\|^{2}_{L^{2}(B_{i})}+\delta^{4}\|\mathbb{N}_{B_{i}}^{(\kappa)}\big[\tilde{\mathrm{E}}_{i}\big]\|^{2}_{L^{2}(B_{i})}+\sum_{n}|\widetilde{\textit{Err}}^{(1)}_{n,B_{j}}|^{2}.

Using the continuity of the Newtonian operator, we then infer

\displaystyle\|\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}(\tilde{\mathrm{E}}_{i})\|^{2}_{L^{2}(B_{i})}\lesssim\|\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}(\tilde{\mathrm{E}}^{\textit{in}})\|^{2}_{L^{2}(B_{i})}+\delta^{4}\ \|\tilde{\mathrm{E}}_{i}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(B_{i})}+\sum_{n}|\widetilde{\textit{Err}}^{(1)}_{n,B_{j}}|^{2}.

(3.44)

Next, we focus on estimating the term
\sum_{n}|\widetilde{\textit{Err}}^{(1)}_{n,B_{j}}|^{2}
. Scaling (
3.43
) to
B_{j}
, we get

\displaystyle\widetilde{\textit{Err}}_{n,B_{j}}

\displaystyle=\delta^{3}\sum_{j\neq i}^{M}\Big\langle\mathbb{G}^{(k)}(z_{i},z_{j})\int_{B_{j}}\tilde{\mathrm{E}}_{j}(\xi)d\xi;\tilde{e}_{n}^{(1)}\Big\rangle+\delta^{4}\sum_{j\neq i}^{M}\Big\langle\int_{0}^{1}\underset{\varsigma}{\nabla}\mathbb{G}^{(k)}(z_{i}+\theta\varsigma,z_{j})\cdot\varsigma d\theta\int_{B_{j}}\tilde{\mathrm{E}}_{j}(\xi)d\xi;\tilde{e}_{n}^{(1)}\Big\rangle

\displaystyle+\delta^{4}\sum_{j\neq i}^{M}\Big\langle\int_{B_{j}}\int_{0}^{1}\underset{\xi}{\nabla}\mathbb{G}^{(k)}(z_{i},z_{j}+\theta\xi)\cdot\xi\tilde{\mathrm{E}}_{j}(\xi)d\theta d\xi;\tilde{e}_{n}^{(1)}\Big\rangle.

Taking the squared modulus and summing over
n
, we obtain the bound

\displaystyle\sum_{n}|\widetilde{\textit{Err}}^{(1)}_{n,B_{j}}|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim M\delta^{6}\Bigg(\sum_{j\neq i}^{M}\Big\|\mathbb{G}^{(k)}(z_{i},z_{j})\int_{B_{j}}\tilde{\mathrm{E}}_{j}(\xi)d\xi\Big\|^{2}_{L^{2}(B_{i})}

\displaystyle+\delta^{2}\sum_{j\neq i}^{M}\Big\|\underset{\varsigma}{\nabla}\mathbb{G}^{(k)}(z_{i}+\theta\varsigma,z_{j})\cdot\varsigma\int_{B_{j}}\tilde{\mathrm{E}}_{j}(\xi)d\xi\Big\|^{2}_{L^{2}(B_{i})}+\delta^{2}\sum_{j\neq i}^{M}\Big\|\underset{\xi}{\nabla}\mathbb{G}^{(k)}(z_{i},z_{j}+\theta\xi)\cdot\xi\tilde{\mathrm{E}}_{j}(\xi)\Big\|^{2}_{L^{2}(B_{i})}\Bigg).

Given that
\mathbb{G}^{(k)}(z_{i},z_{j})=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{|z_{i}-z_{j}|}\right)
, the resulting estimate is as follows

\displaystyle\sum_{n}|\widetilde{\textit{Err}}^{(1)}_{n,B_{j}}|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim M\delta^{6}\ d^{-3}\underset{j}{\max}\|\tilde{\mathrm{E}}_{j}\|_{L^{2}(B_{j})}+M\delta^{8}\ d^{-4}\underset{j}{\max}\|\tilde{\mathrm{E}}_{j}\|_{L^{2}(B_{j})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta^{6-6\lambda}\ \underset{j}{\max}\|\tilde{\mathrm{E}}_{j}\|_{L^{2}(B_{j})}+\delta^{8-7\lambda}\ \underset{j}{\max}\|\tilde{\mathrm{E}}_{j}\|_{L^{2}(B_{j})}.

(3.45)

Finally, using the above estimate (
3.45
) in (
3.44
), we obtain

\displaystyle\|\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}(\tilde{\mathrm{E}}_{i})\|^{2}_{L^{2}(B_{i})}\lesssim\|\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}(\tilde{\mathrm{E}}^{\textit{in}})\|^{2}_{L^{2}(B_{i})}+\delta^{\max\{4,6-6\lambda,8-7\lambda\}}\ \underset{j}{\max}\|\tilde{\mathrm{E}}_{j}\|_{L^{2}(B_{j})}.

(3.46)

2. Projection on
\mathbb{H}_{0}(\textit{curl}\ 0,D_{i}).

Using integration by parts and the Maxwell model (
1.2
), we first find that

\displaystyle\int_{D_{i}}E_{i}\cdot e_{n}^{(2)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{k^{2}}\int_{D_{j}}\delta_{j}^{-1}\,\text{curl}\,\text{curl}\,E_{j}\cdot e_{n}^{(2)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{k^{2}}\int_{D_{j}}\delta_{i}^{-1}\,\text{curl}\,E_{i}\cdot\text{curl}\big(e_{n}^{(2)}\big)\,dx+\frac{1}{k^{2}}\int_{\partial D_{i}}\delta_{i}^{-1}\,\big(\text{curl}\,E_{i}\times e_{n}^{(2)}\big)\cdot\nu\,dS,

which, due to the fact that
\text{curl}\,e_{n}^{(2)}=0
and
e_{n}^{(2)}\times\nu=0
on
\partial D_{i}
, implies that

\displaystyle\overset{2}{\mathbb{P}}(\tilde{\mathrm{E}}_{i})=0,\quad\text{for}\;i=1,2,\ldots,M.

(3.47)

3. Projection on
\nabla\mathbb{H}_{\textit{arm}}.

We note that the Magnetization operator can be decomposed as follows

\displaystyle\mathbb{M}^{(\kappa)}\big[\mathrm{E}\big](\mathrm{x})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{M}^{(\mathrm{0})}\big[\mathrm{E}\big](\mathrm{x})+\frac{k^{2}}{2}\mathbb{N}^{(\mathrm{0})}\big[\mathrm{E}\big](\mathrm{x})+\frac{ik^{3}}{12\pi}\int_{D}\mathrm{E}(\mathrm{y})d\mathrm{y}-\frac{k^{2}}{2}\int_{D}\mathbb{G}^{(\mathrm{0})}(\mathrm{x},\mathrm{y})\frac{\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{x},\mathrm{y})\cdot\mathrm{E}(\mathrm{y})}{\|\mathrm{x}-\mathrm{y}\|^{2}}d\mathrm{y}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4\pi}\sum_{\mathrm{j}\geq 3}\frac{(ik)^{\mathrm{j}+1}}{(\mathrm{j}+1)!}\int_{D}\underset{\mathrm{x}}{\textit{Hess}}(\|\mathrm{x}-\mathrm{y}\|^{\mathrm{j}})\cdot\mathrm{E}(\mathrm{y})d\mathrm{y},

where
\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{x},\mathrm{y}):=(\mathrm{x}-\mathrm{y})\otimes(\mathrm{x}-\mathrm{y})
. Using this decomposition, we can rewrite the Lippmann-Schwinger equation (
3.17
) as follows

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}_{i}(\mathrm{x})+\eta\;\mathbb{M}_{D_{i}}^{(0)}\big[\mathrm{E}_{i}\big](\mathrm{x})=\mathrm{E}^{\textit{in}}(\mathrm{x})+\eta\sum_{j\neq m}^{M}\Big(-\mathbb{M}_{D_{j}}^{(\kappa)}\big[\mathrm{E}_{j}\big](\mathrm{x})+k^{2}\mathbb{N}_{D_{j}}^{(\kappa)}\Big)\big[\mathrm{E}_{i}\big](\mathrm{x})+k^{2}\eta\;\mathbb{N}_{D_{j}}^{(\kappa)}\big[\mathrm{E}_{i}\big](\mathrm{x})-\textit{Err}_{1,D_{i}},

(3.48)

where,

\displaystyle\textit{Err}_{1,D_{i}}

\displaystyle:=\frac{k^{2}}{2}\mathbb{N}_{D_{i}}^{(\mathrm{0})}\big[\mathrm{E}\big](\mathrm{x})+\frac{ik^{3}}{12\pi}\int_{D_{i}}\mathrm{E}(\mathrm{y})d\mathrm{y}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}-}\frac{k^{2}}{2}\int_{D_{i}}\mathbb{G}^{(\mathrm{0})}(\mathrm{x},\mathrm{y})\frac{\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{x},\mathrm{y})\cdot\mathrm{E}(\mathrm{y})}{\|\mathrm{x}-\mathrm{y}\|^{2}}d\mathrm{y}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4\pi}\sum_{\mathrm{j}\geq 3}\frac{(ik)^{\mathrm{j}+1}}{(\mathrm{j}+1)!}\int_{D_{i}}\underset{\mathrm{x}}{\textit{Hess}}(\|\mathrm{x}-\mathrm{y}\|^{\mathrm{j}})\cdot\mathrm{E}(\mathrm{y})d\mathrm{y}.

(3.49)

Next, we scale the domain
D_{i}
to
B_{i}
, leading to the following expression for the error term

\displaystyle\widetilde{\textit{Err}}_{1,B_{i}}

\displaystyle=\frac{k^{2}}{2}\delta^{2}\mathbb{N}_{B_{i}}^{(\mathrm{0})}\big[\tilde{\mathrm{E}}_{i}\big](\mathrm{x})+\frac{ik^{3}}{12\pi}\delta^{3}\int_{B_{i}}\tilde{\mathrm{E}}_{i}(\xi)d\xi-\frac{k^{2}}{2}\delta^{2}\int_{B_{i}}\mathbb{G}^{(\mathrm{0})}(\varsigma,\xi)\frac{\mathrm{A}(\varsigma,\xi)\cdot\tilde{\mathrm{E}}_{i}(\xi)}{\|\varsigma-\xi\|^{2}}d\xi

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4\pi}\sum_{\mathrm{j}\geq 3}\frac{(ik)^{\mathrm{j}+1}}{(\mathrm{j}+1)!}\delta^{j+1}\int_{B_{i}}\underset{\varsigma}{\textit{Hess}}(\|\varsigma-\xi\|^{\mathrm{j}})\cdot\tilde{\mathrm{E}}_{i}(\xi)d\xi.

(3.50)

We also recall that for
x\in D_{i}

\displaystyle\Big(-\mathbb{M}_{D_{j}}^{(\kappa)}+k^{2}\mathbb{N}_{D_{j}}^{(\kappa)}\Big)\big[\mathrm{E}_{j}\big](\mathrm{x})=\int_{D_{j}}\bm{\Upsilon}^{(k)}(x,y)\ E_{j}(y)dy,

(3.51)

where,
\bm{\Upsilon}^{(k)}(x,y):=\underset{x}{\textit{Hess}}\mathcal{G}^{(k)}(x,y)+k^{2}\mathcal{G}^{(k)}(x,y)\mathbb{I}
represents the corresponding dyadic Green’s function, and
\mathcal{G}^{(k)}(x,y)
is the Green’s function for the Helmholtz operator. Consequently, we can deduce the following relation

\displaystyle E_{i}+\eta\mathbb{M}_{D_{i}}^{(0)}\big[E_{i}\big](x)

\displaystyle=E^{\textit{in}}+\eta\sum_{j\neq i}^{M}\bm{\Upsilon}^{(k)}(z_{i},z_{j})\int_{D_{j}}E_{j}(y)dy

\displaystyle+\eta\sum_{j\neq i}^{M}\int_{0}^{1}\underset{x}{\nabla}\bm{\Upsilon}^{(k)}(z_{i}+\theta(x-z_{i}),z_{j})\cdot(x-z_{i})d\theta\int_{D_{j}}E_{j}(y)dy

\displaystyle+\eta\sum_{j\neq i}^{M}\int_{D_{j}}\int_{0}^{1}\underset{y}{\nabla}\bm{\Upsilon}^{(k)}(z_{i},z_{j}+\theta(x-z_{j}))\cdot(y-z_{j})E_{j}(y)d\theta dy+k^{2}\eta\;\mathbb{N}_{D_{i}}^{(\kappa)}\big[E_{i}\big](\mathrm{x})

\displaystyle-\textit{Err}_{1,D_{i}}.

Next, we take the projection of equation (
3.48
) onto
\nabla\mathbb{H}_{\textit{arm}}
, and after appropriate scaling, we obtain

\displaystyle\big\langle\tilde{\mathrm{E}}_{i},\tilde{e}_{n}^{(3)}\big\rangle

\displaystyle+\eta\;\big\langle\mathbb{M}_{B_{i}}^{(0)}\big[\tilde{\mathrm{E}}_{i}\big],\tilde{e}_{n}^{(3)}\big\rangle=\big\langle\tilde{\mathrm{E}}^{\textit{in}},\tilde{e}_{n}^{(3)}\big\rangle+\eta\;\delta^{3}\sum_{j\neq m}^{M}\big\langle\bm{\Upsilon}^{(k)}(z_{i},z_{j})\cdot\int_{B_{j}}\tilde{\mathrm{E}}_{j}(\xi)d\xi,\tilde{e}_{n}^{(3)}\big\rangle

\displaystyle+\eta\;\delta^{4}\sum_{j\neq m}^{M}\big\langle\int_{0}^{1}\underset{x}{\nabla}\bm{\Upsilon}^{(k)}(z_{i}+\theta\delta\varsigma,z_{j})\cdot\varsigma d\theta\int_{B_{j}}\tilde{\mathrm{E}}_{j}(\xi)d\xi,\tilde{e}_{n}^{(3)}\big\rangle

\displaystyle+\eta\;\delta^{4}\sum_{j\neq m}^{M}\big\langle\int_{B_{j}}\int_{0}^{1}\underset{y}{\nabla}\bm{\Upsilon}^{(k)}(z_{m},z_{j}+\theta\delta\xi)\cdot\xi\tilde{\mathrm{E}}_{j})(\xi)\ d\theta d\xi,\tilde{e}_{n}^{(3)}\big\rangle+k^{2}\eta\;\delta^{2}\big\langle\mathbb{N}_{B_{i}}^{(\kappa)}\big[\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}(\tilde{\mathrm{E}}_{i})\big](\mathrm{x}),\tilde{e}_{n}^{(3)}\big\rangle

\displaystyle-\big\langle\widetilde{\textit{Err}}_{1,B_{i}},\tilde{e}_{n}^{(3)}\big\rangle.

(3.52)

Furthermore, by solving the dispersion equation
f_{n_{0}}(\omega,\gamma):=1+\eta\lambda_{n}^{(3)}=0
, we establish a property based on the choice of the incident frequency and the Lorentz model, which yields the following condition for
\mathrm{h}>0

\displaystyle\big|1+\eta\lambda^{(3)}_{n}\big|\sim\begin{cases}\delta^{\mathrm{h}}&n=n_{0}\\
1&n\neq n_{0}.\end{cases}

Based on the discussion in
[
25
, Subsection 2.1]
, we utilize the property that

\displaystyle\Big\|\big(\mathbb{I}+\eta\mathbb{M}_{B_{i}}^{(0)}\Big)^{-1}\Big\|_{\mathbcal{L}\big(L^{2}(B_{i});L^{2}(B_{i})\big)}\lesssim\delta^{-h}.

The sub-space
\nabla\mathbb{H}_{\textit{arm}}
is an invariant subspace of the operator
\mathbb{M}_{B_{i}}^{(0)}
, where it induces a complete orthonormal basis
\big(\lambda^{(3)}_{\mathrm{n}},\tilde{\mathrm{e}}^{(3)}_{\mathrm{n}}\big)_{\mathrm{n}\in\mathbb{N}}.
After taking the squared modulus and summing over
n
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\big\|\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}(\tilde{\mathrm{E}}_{i})\big\|^{2}_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\mathrm{B}_{i})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta^{-2h}\Bigg(\big\|\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}(\tilde{\mathrm{E}}^{\textit{in}})\big\|^{2}_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\mathrm{B}_{i})}+\delta^{6}\sum_{j\neq i}^{M}\Big\|\bm{\Upsilon}^{(k)}(z_{i},z_{j})\cdot\int_{B_{j}}\tilde{\mathrm{E}}_{j}(\xi)d\xi\Big\|^{2}_{L^{2}(B_{i})}

\displaystyle+\delta^{6}\sum_{j\neq m}^{M}\big|\bm{\Upsilon}^{(k)}(z_{m},z_{j})\cdot\int_{B_{j}}\tilde{\mathrm{E}}_{j})(\xi)d\xi\big|\sum_{r>j,r\neq j}^{M}\big|\bm{\Upsilon}^{(k)}(z_{i},z_{r})\cdot\int_{B_{j}}\tilde{\mathrm{E}}_{j}(\xi)d\xi\big|

\displaystyle+M\delta^{8}\sum_{j\neq i}^{M}\Big\|\int_{0}^{1}\underset{\varsigma}{\nabla}\bm{\Upsilon}^{(k)}(z_{i}+\theta\delta\varsigma,z_{j})d\theta\cdot\varsigma\int_{B_{j}}\tilde{\mathrm{E}}_{j}(\xi)\ d\xi\Big\|_{L^{2}(B_{i})}^{2}

\displaystyle+M\delta^{8}\sum_{j\neq i}^{M}\Big\|\int_{B_{j}}\int_{0}^{1}\underset{\xi}{\nabla}\bm{\Upsilon}^{(k)}(z_{i},z_{j}+\theta\delta\xi)\cdot\xi\tilde{\mathrm{E}}_{j}(\xi)\ d\theta d\xi\Big\|_{L^{2}(B_{i})}^{2}+\delta^{4}\|\mathbb{N}_{B_{i}}^{(\kappa)}\big[\tilde{\mathrm{E}}_{i})\big]\|^{2}_{L^{2}(B_{i})}

\displaystyle+\|\widetilde{\textit{Err}}_{1,B_{i}}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(B_{i})}\Bigg).

(3.53)

We can express the contributions from various terms. For instance, using the fact that
\bm{\Upsilon}(z_{i},z_{j})\simeq d_{ij}^{-3},
we deduce

\displaystyle\delta^{6}\sum_{j\neq i}^{M}\Big\|\bm{\Upsilon}^{(k)}(z_{i},z_{j})\cdot\int_{B_{j}}\tilde{\mathrm{E}}_{j}(\xi)d\xi\Big\|^{2}_{L^{2}(B_{i})}\lesssim\sum_{j\neq i}^{M}d_{ij}^{-6}\|\tilde{E}_{j}\|_{L^{2}(B_{j})}\lesssim d^{-6}\underset{j}{\max}\|\tilde{E}_{j}\|_{L^{2}(B_{j})}.

(3.54)

Moreover, for the mixed term, we have

\displaystyle\delta^{6}\sum_{j\neq m}^{M}\big|\bm{\Upsilon}^{(k)}(z_{m},z_{j})\cdot\int_{B_{j}}\tilde{\mathrm{E}}_{j}(\xi)d\xi\big|\sum_{r>j,r\neq j}^{M}\big|\bm{\Upsilon}^{(k)}(z_{i},z_{r})\cdot\int_{B_{j}}\tilde{\mathrm{E}}_{j}(\xi)d\xi\big|

\displaystyle\leq\delta^{6}\sum_{j\neq i}^{M}d_{ij}^{-6}\|\tilde{E}_{j}\|_{L^{2}(B_{j})}\sum_{j\neq i}^{M}d_{ij}^{-6}\|\tilde{E}_{j}\|_{L^{2}(B_{j})}\lesssim\delta^{6}d^{-6}|\log(\delta)|^{2}\underset{j}{\max}\|\tilde{E}_{j})\|_{L^{2}(B_{j})}.

(3.55)

We now, estimate the following term.

\displaystyle M\delta^{8-2h}\sum_{j\neq i}^{M}\Big\|\int_{0}^{1}\underset{\varsigma}{\nabla}\bm{\Upsilon}^{(k)}(z_{i}+\theta\delta\varsigma,z_{j})d\theta\cdot\varsigma\int_{B_{j}}\tilde{\mathrm{E}}_{j}(\xi)\ d\xi\Big\|_{L^{2}(B_{i})}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim M\delta^{8-2h}\sum_{j\neq i}^{M}d_{ij}^{-8}\Big\|\tilde{\mathrm{E}}_{j}\Big\|_{L^{2}(B_{i})}^{2}\lesssim\delta^{8-11\lambda-2h}\underset{j}{\max}\big\|\tilde{\mathrm{E}}_{j}\big\|^{2}_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\mathrm{B}_{j})}.

(3.56)

In a similar way, we have

\displaystyle M\delta^{8}\sum_{j\neq i}^{M}\Big\|\int_{B_{j}}\int_{0}^{1}\underset{\xi}{\nabla}\bm{\Upsilon}^{(k)}(z_{i},z_{j}+\theta\delta\xi)\cdot\xi\tilde{\mathrm{E}}_{j}(\xi)\ d\theta d\xi\Big\|_{L^{2}(B_{i})}^{2}\lesssim\delta^{8-11\lambda-2h}\underset{j}{\max}\big\|\tilde{\mathrm{E}}_{j}\big\|^{2}_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\mathrm{B}_{j})}.

(3.57)

Considering the expression (
3.50
), we observe that the dominating term is
\frac{k^{2}}{2}\delta^{2}\mathbb{N}_{B_{i}}^{(\mathrm{0})}\big[\tilde{\mathrm{E}}_{i}\big](\mathrm{x}).
Therefore, neglecting the other higher order terms, we take the
\mathrm{L}^{2}(B_{i})
-norm, and utilizing the continuity of the Newtonian operator, we obtain

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{\textit{Err}}_{1,B_{i}}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(B_{i})}\lesssim\delta^{4}\big\|\tilde{\mathrm{E}}_{j}\big\|^{2}_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\mathrm{B}_{j})}.

(3.58)

It is noteworthy that
\nabla\bm{\Upsilon}^{(\mathrm{0})}(z_{m},z_{j})\big|\simeq\frac{1}{d^{4}_{mj}}.

Subsequently, by leveraging the continuity of the Newtonian operator and combining the estimates from (
3.54
), (
3.55
), (
3.56
), (
3.57
), (
3.58
), and we substitute these findings into (
3.53
), leading us to the conclusion that

\displaystyle\big\|\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}(\tilde{\mathrm{E}}_{i})\big\|^{2}_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\mathrm{B}_{i})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta^{-2h}\big\|\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}(\tilde{\mathrm{E}}^{\textit{in}})\big\|^{2}_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\mathrm{B}_{i})}+\delta^{8-11\lambda-2h}\underset{j}{\max}\big\|\tilde{\mathrm{E}}_{j}\big\|^{2}_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\mathrm{B}_{j})}

\displaystyle+\delta^{6}\underset{j}{\max}\|\tilde{E}_{j}\|_{L^{2}(B_{j})}+\delta^{6}d^{-6}|\log(\delta)|^{2}\underset{j}{\max}\|\tilde{E}_{j}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(B_{j})}+\delta^{4-2h}\|\tilde{\mathrm{E}}_{j}\big\|^{2}_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\mathrm{B}_{j})}.

(3.59)

Now, we use Parseval’s identity to estimate
\tilde{E}_{i}
, i.e. we write

\big\|\tilde{E}_{i}\big\|^{2}_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\mathrm{B}_{i})}=\sum\limits_{\mathrm{j}=1}^{3}\big\|\overset{\mathrm{j}}{\mathbb{P}}(\tilde{E}_{i})\big\|^{2}_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\mathrm{B}_{i})}.
Subsequently, due to the estimate (
3.46
), (
3.47
) and (
3.59
), we derive that

\displaystyle\underset{i}{\max}\|\tilde{E}_{i}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(B_{i})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta^{-2h}\big\|\tilde{\mathrm{E}}^{\textit{in}}\big\|^{2}_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\mathrm{B}_{i})}+\delta^{\{\max(4,4-2h,8-11\lambda-2h,6-6\lambda,8-7\lambda\}}\underset{j}{\max}\|\tilde{E}_{j}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(B_{j})}

\displaystyle+\delta^{6}\underset{j}{\max}\|\tilde{E}_{j}\|_{L^{2}(B_{j})}+\delta^{6}d^{-6}|\log(\delta)|^{2}\underset{j}{\max}\|\tilde{E}_{j}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(B_{j})}

Thus, according to the invertibility condition of the linear algebraic system, it follows that
\lambda\leq 1-\frac{h}{3}.
Looking at the above expression, we examine the case where

\displaystyle\frac{9}{5}<h<2,

and therefore, we derive

\displaystyle\underset{i}{\max}\big\|\tilde{\mathrm{E}}_{i}\big\|^{2}_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\mathrm{B}_{i})}\lesssim\delta^{-2h}\big\|\tilde{\mathrm{E}}^{\textit{in}}\big\|^{2}_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\mathrm{B}_{i})},\;\text{which implies,}\;\underset{i}{\max}\big\|\mathrm{E}_{i}\big\|^{2}_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(D_{i})}\lesssim\delta^{3-2h}.

This concludes the proof of the initial estimate stated in Proposition
2.2
.

Part 2: Proof of the Estimate for
\big\|\mathrm{E}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{4}}(D)}
in Proposition
2.2

We start by recalling the Lippmann-Schwinger (LS) equation, which provides the solution to the electromagnetic scattering problem (
1.2
) for
i=1,2,\ldots,M

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}_{i}(\mathrm{x})+\eta\sum_{i=1}^{M}\mathbb{M}_{D_{i}}^{(k)}\big[\mathrm{E}_{i}\big](\mathrm{x})-k^{2}\eta\sum_{i=1}^{M}\mathbb{N}_{D_{i}}^{(k)}\big[\mathrm{E}_{i}\big](\mathrm{x})=\mathrm{E}^{\textit{in}}(\mathrm{x}),\quad\text{for}\ x\in D:=\bigcup_{i=1}^{M}D_{i},

(3.60)

Now, with integration by parts and as
\nabla\cdot\mathrm{E}_{i}=0
, we show that
\mathbb{M}_{D_{i}}^{(k)}\big[\mathrm{E}_{i}\big]=\nabla\mathcal{S}_{\partial D_{i}}^{(k)}\big[\nu\cdot\mathrm{E}_{i}\big]
, where
\mathcal{S}_{\partial D_{i}}^{(k)}
is the Single-Layer operator defined from
\mathbb{L}^{2}(\partial D_{i})
to
\mathbb{H}^{\frac{3}{2}}(D_{i})
, by

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{\partial D_{i}}^{(k)}\big[f\big](\cdot):=\int_{\partial D_{i}}\mathbb{G}^{(k)}(\cdot,\mathrm{y})\;f(\mathrm{y})d\sigma_{\mathrm{y}}.

Therefore, with the help of the above identity, we rewrite the equation (
3.60
) for
x\in D_{i}
as follows

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}_{i}(\mathrm{x})+\eta\nabla\mathcal{S}_{\partial D_{i}}^{(k)}\big[\nu\cdot\mathrm{E}_{i}\big](\mathrm{x})+\eta\sum_{j\neq i}^{M}\nabla\mathcal{S}_{\partial D_{j}}^{(k)}\big[\nu\cdot\mathrm{E}_{j}\big](\mathrm{x})-k^{2}\eta\mathbb{N}_{D_{i}}^{(k)}\big[\mathrm{E}_{i}\big](\mathrm{x})-k^{2}\eta\sum_{j\neq i}^{M}\mathbb{N}_{D_{j}}^{(k)}\big[\mathrm{E}_{j}\big](\mathrm{x})=\mathrm{E}^{\textit{in}}(\mathrm{x}).

(3.61)

Scaling to
B
and taking
curl
in above equation, we arrive at

\displaystyle\textit{curl}(\tilde{E}_{i})=\textit{curl}(\tilde{E}^{\textit{in}})+k^{2}\eta\delta^{2}\textit{curl}\ \mathbb{N}_{B_{i}}^{(k\delta)}\big[\tilde{\mathrm{E}}_{i}\big]+\widetilde{\textit{Err}}_{\textit{f},1},

(3.62)

where we define

\displaystyle\widetilde{\textit{Err}}_{\textit{f},1}

\displaystyle:=\delta^{3}\sum_{j\neq i}^{M}\mathbb{G}^{(k)}(z_{i},z_{j})\int_{B_{j}}\textit{curl}(\tilde{\mathrm{E}}_{j})(\xi)d\xi+\delta^{4}\sum_{j\neq i}^{M}\int_{0}^{1}\underset{\varsigma}{\nabla}\mathbb{G}^{(k)}(z_{i}+\theta\varsigma,z_{j})\cdot\varsigma d\theta\int_{B_{j}}\textit{curl}(\tilde{\mathrm{E}}_{j})(\xi)d\xi

\displaystyle+\delta^{4}\sum_{j\neq i}^{M}\int_{B_{j}}\int_{0}^{1}\underset{\xi}{\nabla}\mathbb{G}^{(k)}(z_{i},z_{j}+\theta\xi)\cdot\xi\textit{curl}(\tilde{\mathrm{E}}_{j})(\xi)d\theta d\xi.

(3.63)

Taking the
L^{2}(B_{i})
-norm to both hand side of the above equation yields to

\displaystyle\|\textit{curl}(\tilde{E}_{i})\|_{L^{2}(B_{i})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\textit{curl}(\tilde{E}^{\textit{in}})\|_{L^{2}(B_{i})}+\delta^{2}\|\textit{curl}\ \mathbb{N}_{B_{i}}^{(k\delta)}\big[\tilde{\mathrm{E}}_{i}\big]\|_{L^{2}(B_{i})}+\|\widetilde{\textit{Err}}_{\textit{f},1}\|_{L^{2}(B_{i})}.

Next, we use the continuity of Newtonian operator, the fact that
|\mathbb{G}^{(k)}(z_{i},z_{j})|\sim d_{ij}^{-1}
and
\nabla\mathbb{G}^{(k)}(z_{i},z_{j})\sim d_{ij}^{-2}
to deduce the following

\displaystyle\Big(1-\delta^{\max\{3-3\lambda,4-3\lambda\}}\Big)\|\textit{curl}(\tilde{E}_{i})\|_{L^{2}(B_{i})}\lesssim\|\textit{curl}(\tilde{E}^{\textit{in}})\|_{L^{2}(B_{i})}+\delta^{2}\|\tilde{E}_{i}\|_{L^{2}(B_{i})}.

(3.64)

Due to the a-priori estimate
\|\tilde{E}_{i}\|_{L^{2}(B_{i})}\sim\delta^{-h},
and based on the chosen regime

\displaystyle\lambda\leq 1-\frac{h}{3},\;\text{with}\;\frac{9}{5}<h<2,

it implies that

\displaystyle\|\textit{curl}(\tilde{E}_{i})\|_{L^{2}(B_{i})}\lesssim 1.

(3.65)

We also have the following estimate

\displaystyle\big\|\nu\cdot\tilde{\mathrm{E}}_{i}\big\|_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\partial B_{i})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\big\|\tilde{\mathrm{E}}_{i}\big\|_{H(\textit{curl},B_{i})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Big(\big\|\tilde{\mathrm{E}}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\mathrm{B})}^{2}+\big\|\textit{curl}\;\tilde{\mathrm{E}}_{i}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(B_{i})}^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\sim\delta^{-\mathrm{h}}.

(3.66)

To write the closed system of equations, we need to take the normal trace of the equation (
3.61
) in
\mathrm{B}
and use the jump relation of the single-layer operator to get

\displaystyle\big(1+\frac{\eta}{2}\big)\nu\cdot\tilde{\mathrm{E}}_{i}=-\eta\mathcal{K}_{\partial B_{i}}^{*}(\nu\cdot\tilde{\mathrm{E}}_{i})+k^{2}\eta\delta^{2}\ \nu\cdot\mathbb{N}_{B_{i}}^{(k\delta)}\big[\tilde{\mathrm{E}}_{i}\big]+\eta\widetilde{\textit{Err}}_{\textit{f},3}+k^{2}\eta\ \nu\cdot\widetilde{\textit{Err}}_{\textit{f},2}+\nu\cdot\tilde{\mathrm{E}}^{\textit{in}},

(3.67)

where,
\mathcal{K}_{\partial B_{i}}^{*}
is the Neumann-Poincaré operator defined by

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}^{*}_{\partial B_{i}}\big[f\big](x):=\text{p.v.}\int_{\partial B_{i}}\partial_{\nu_{\mathrm{x}}}\mathbb{G}^{(\kappa)}(\cdot,\mathrm{y})\;f(\mathrm{y})d\sigma_{\mathrm{y}}.

We also define

\displaystyle\widetilde{\textit{Err}}_{\textit{f},3}

\displaystyle:=-\sum_{j\neq i}^{M}\partial_{\nu}\mathcal{S}_{\partial D_{j}}^{(k)}\big[\nu\cdot\mathrm{E}_{j}\big]

\displaystyle=-\eta\delta^{2}\Bigg(\sum_{j\neq i}^{M}\partial_{\nu}\mathbb{G}^{(k)}(z_{i},z_{j})\int_{\partial B_{j}}\nu\cdot\tilde{\mathrm{E}}_{j}(\xi)d\sigma_{\xi}+\delta^{4}\sum_{j\neq i}^{M}\int_{0}^{1}\underset{\varsigma}{\nabla}\partial_{\nu}\mathbb{G}^{(k)}(z_{i}+\theta\varsigma,z_{j})\cdot\varsigma d\theta\int_{\partial B_{j}}\nu\cdot\tilde{\mathrm{E}}_{j}(\xi)d\sigma_{\xi}

\displaystyle+\delta^{4}\sum_{j\neq i}^{M}\int_{\partial B_{j}}\int_{0}^{1}\underset{\xi}{\nabla}\partial_{\nu}\mathbb{G}^{(k)}(z_{i},z_{j}+\theta\xi)\cdot\xi\nu\cdot\tilde{\mathrm{E}}_{j}(\xi)d\theta d\sigma_{\xi}\Bigg)

(3.68)

and

\displaystyle\widetilde{\textit{Err}}_{\textit{f},2}

\displaystyle:=\delta^{3}\sum_{j\neq i}^{M}\mathbb{G}^{(k)}(z_{i},z_{j})\int_{B_{j}}\tilde{\mathrm{E}}_{j}(\xi)d\xi+\delta^{4}\sum_{j\neq i}^{M}\int_{0}^{1}\underset{\varsigma}{\nabla}\mathbb{G}^{(k)}(z_{i}+\theta\varsigma,z_{j})\cdot\varsigma d\theta\int_{B_{j}}\tilde{\mathrm{E}}_{j}(\xi)d\xi

\displaystyle+\delta^{4}\sum_{j\neq i}^{M}\int_{B_{j}}\int_{0}^{1}\underset{\xi}{\nabla}\mathbb{G}^{(k)}(z_{i},z_{j}+\theta\xi)\cdot\xi\tilde{\mathrm{E}}_{j}(\xi)d\theta d\xi

(3.69)

Consequently, taking the
H^{\frac{1}{2}}(\partial B_{i})
-norm on both sides of the aforementioned equation (
3.67
), we see that

\displaystyle\|\nu\cdot\tilde{E}_{i}\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}(\partial B_{i})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Big|\frac{1}{1+\eta}\Big|\|\nu\cdot\tilde{\mathrm{E}}^{\textit{in}}\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}(\partial B_{i})}+\Big|\frac{\eta}{1+\eta}\Big|\|\mathcal{K}_{\partial B_{i}}^{*}(\nu\cdot\tilde{\mathrm{E}}_{i})\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}(\partial B_{i})}+\delta^{2}\Big|\frac{\eta}{1+\eta}\Big|\|\nu\cdot\mathbb{N}_{B_{i}}^{(k\delta)}\big[\tilde{\mathrm{E}}_{i}\big]\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}(\partial B_{i})}

\displaystyle+\Big|\frac{\eta}{1+\eta}\Big|\|\widetilde{\textit{Err}}_{\textit{f},3}\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}(\partial B_{i})}+\Big|\frac{\eta}{1+\eta}\Big|\|\widetilde{\textit{Err}}_{\textit{f},2}\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}(\partial B_{i})}.

(3.70)

We know that for the case when
\partial B_{i}
is
\mathcal{C}^{2}
-regular, the Neumann-Poincaré operator
\mathcal{K}_{\partial B_{i}}^{*}
is continuous from
H^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\partial B_{i})\to H^{\frac{1}{2}}(\partial B_{i}).
Then, using the continuity of the Newtonian operator and due to the estimate (
3.66
), we arrive at the following reduced expression

\displaystyle\|\nu\cdot\tilde{E}_{i}\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}(\partial B_{i})}\lesssim 1+\delta^{-h}+\delta^{2-h}+\|\widetilde{\textit{Err}}_{\textit{f},3}\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}(\partial B_{i})}+\|\widetilde{\textit{Err}}_{\textit{f},3}\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}(\partial B_{i})}.

(3.71)

Then, we use the fact that
|\partial_{\nu}\mathbb{G}^{(k)}(z_{i},z_{j})|\sim d_{ij}^{-2}
and
\nabla\partial_{\nu}\mathbb{G}^{(k)}(z_{i},z_{j})\sim d_{ij}^{-3}
to deduce the following

\displaystyle\Big(1-\delta^{2-3\lambda}\Big)\|\nu\cdot\tilde{E}_{i}\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}(\partial B_{i})}\lesssim\delta^{-h}+\delta^{3-3\lambda-h}.

Therefore, based on the chosen regime

\displaystyle\lambda\leq 1-\frac{h}{3},\;\text{with}\;\frac{9}{5}<h<2,

it follows from the previous estimate that

\displaystyle\|\nu\cdot\tilde{E}_{i}\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}(\partial B_{i})}\lesssim\delta^{-h}.

(3.72)

Then, based on the inequality as shown in
[
3
, ineq. 1.4]
, we deduce

\displaystyle\big\|\tilde{\mathrm{E}}_{i}\big\|_{H^{1}(B)}\lesssim\underbrace{\big\|\tilde{\mathrm{E}}_{i}\big\|_{L^{2}(B)}}_{\sim\;\delta^{-\mathrm{h}}}+\underbrace{\big\|\textit{curl}(\tilde{\mathrm{E}}_{i})\big\|_{L^{2}(B)}}_{\sim\;1}+\underbrace{\big\|\text{div}\;\tilde{\mathrm{E}}\big\|_{L^{2}(B)}}_{=\;0}+\big\|\nu\cdot\tilde{\mathrm{E}}\big\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}(\partial B)}\sim\delta^{-\mathrm{h}}.

(3.73)

We have the following estimate based on Gagliardo-Nirenberg inequality, estimate
\|\tilde{E}_{i}\|_{L^{2}(B_{i})}\sim\delta^{-h},
and using (
3.73
)

\displaystyle\big\|\tilde{\mathrm{E}}_{i}\big\|_{L^{\mathrm{4}}(\mathrm{B})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\big\|\tilde{\mathrm{E}}_{i}\big\|_{L^{\mathrm{2}}(B_{i})}^{\frac{1}{2}}\big\|\tilde{\mathrm{E}}_{i}\big\|_{H^{1}(B)}^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta^{-\frac{\mathrm{h}}{2}}\cdot\delta^{-\frac{\mathrm{h}}{2}}\sim\delta^{-\mathrm{h}}.

(3.74)

So, using the aforementioned estimate and scaling it back to
D_{i}
, we arrive at

\displaystyle\big\|E_{i}\big\|_{L^{\mathrm{4}}(D_{i})}\sim\delta^{\frac{3}{4}-\mathrm{h}}.

This completes the proof of Proposition
2.2
.
∎ To complete the proof of the Lemma
3.3
, we first recall the derived linear algebraic system given by (
3.40
)

\displaystyle\int_{D_{i}}\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}(E_{i})(y)\ dy

\displaystyle-\eta\sum_{j\neq i}^{M}\mathbcal{P}_{D_{i}}\cdot\bm{\Upsilon}^{(k)}(z_{i},z_{j})\int_{D_{j}}\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}(E_{j})(y)dy=\mathbcal{P}_{D_{i}}\cdot\mathrm{E}^{\textit{in}}(z_{i})+\mathcal{O}\Big(\delta^{4-h}\Big)

\displaystyle+\mathcal{O}\Big(\delta^{\frac{11}{2}-h}d^{-4}\underset{j}{\max}\|\overset{3}{\mathbb{P}}(E_{j})\|_{L^{2}(D_{j})}\Big)+\mathcal{O}\Big(\delta^{\frac{11}{2}-h}d^{-2}\underset{j}{\max}\|\overset{1}{\mathbb{P}}(E_{j})\|_{L^{2}(D_{j})}\Big).

(3.75)

From the a priori estimates, we have

\displaystyle\underset{i}{\max}\big\|\mathrm{E}_{i}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(D_{i})}\lesssim\delta^{\frac{3}{2}-h}.

Consequently, we obtain the following linear algebraic system

\displaystyle\mathbcal{Q}_{i}-\eta\sum_{j\neq i}^{M}\mathbcal{P}_{D_{i}}\cdot\bm{\Upsilon}^{(k)}(z_{i},z_{j})\cdot\mathbcal{Q}_{j}=\mathbcal{P}_{D_{i}}\cdot\mathrm{E}^{\textit{in}}(z_{i})+\mathcal{O}\Big(\delta^{\min\{4-h;7-2h-4\lambda\}}\Big),\;\texttt{for}\ i=1,2,\ldots,M.

Here, we define the polarization matrix
\mathbcal{P}_{D_{i}}
as

\displaystyle\mathbcal{P}_{D_{i}}=\delta^{3}\sum_{n}\frac{1}{1+\eta\lambda_{n}^{(3)}}\int_{B_{i}}e^{(3)}_{n}(\xi)d\xi\otimes\int_{B_{i}}e^{(3)}_{n}(\xi)d\xi.

The above algebraic system is invertible under the following condition, as discussed in
[
8
, Section 6.1]
:

\displaystyle|\eta|\underset{j=1,2,\ldots,M}{\max}\|\mathbcal{P}_{D_{i}}\|\ d^{-3}<1.

(3.76)

This condition ensures the uniqueness and existence of solutions to the system, thereby completing the proof of the lemma.
∎ Neglecting the error order term, let us now rewrite the above algebraic system, which will be useful in defining its continuous effective equation in the next section, as follows:

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbcal{Q}}_{i}-\eta\sum_{j\neq i}^{M}\bm{\Upsilon}^{(k)}(z_{i},z_{j})\cdot\mathbcal{P}_{D_{j}}\cdot\tilde{\mathbcal{Q}}_{j}=\mathrm{E}^{\textit{in}}(z_{i}),

(3.77)

where, we set
\tilde{\mathbcal{Q}}_{i}:=\mathbcal{P}^{-1}_{D_{i}}\cdot\mathbcal{Q}_{i}.
Then, due to the properties (
1.15
)–(
1.16
),
\mathbcal{P}_{D_{i}}
is the polarization matrix given by

\displaystyle\mathbcal{P}_{D_{i}}=\frac{\delta^{3}}{1+\eta\lambda_{n_{0}}^{(3)}}\sum_{m=1}^{m_{n_{0}}}\int_{B_{i}}e^{(3)}_{m,n_{0}}(B)\otimes\int_{B_{i}}e^{(3)}_{m,n_{0}}(B)+\mathcal{O}(\delta^{3}),

(3.78)

where
\tilde{e}^{(3)}_{m,n_{0}}
representing the scalled eigenfunctions associated with the space
\nabla\mathbb{H}_{\textit{arm}}
on the domain
B_{i}
and
\displaystyle e^{(3)}_{m,n_{0}}(B)=\frac{1}{\|\tilde{e}_{m,n_{0}}^{(3)}\|_{L^{2}(B_{i})}}\tilde{e}_{m,n_{0}}^{(3)}
representing the corresponding normalized functions. Subsequently, using the same properties (
1.15
)–(
1.16
), we can deduce that
\mathbcal{P}_{D_{i}}=\delta^{3-h}\mathbcal{P}_{B}
, where, we have
\mathbcal{P}_{B}
as

\displaystyle\mathbcal{P}_{B}:=C_{B}\sum_{m=1}^{m_{n_{0}}}\int_{B_{i}}e^{(3)}_{m,n_{0}}(B)\otimes\int_{B_{i}}e^{(3)}_{m,n_{0}}(B),\;\text{with}\ C_{B}=\Bigg(\lambda_{n_{0}}^{(3)}\cdot\dfrac{k_{p}^{2}k_{n_{0}}\sqrt{5}}{k_{n_{0}}^{4}+(\zeta_{n_{0}}k_{n_{0}})^{2}}\Bigg)^{-1}.

(3.79)

Let us now discuss about its corresponding effective equation in the next section.

3.2.3
The Effective Medium Theory for the Electromagnetic Problem

Now, we distribute the plasmonic nanoparticle periodically inside a given smooth domain
\mathbf{\Omega}
with a period given by
\delta^{1-\frac{h}{3}}
, which means that the distance between close nanoparticle is of the order
\delta^{1-\frac{h}{3}}
. For simplicity, we take the nanoparticles having all the same contrasts. In this case, we show that the algebric system derived in (
3.77
)-(
3.78
) along with the invertibility condition (
3.76
) "converges" to the solution of the following kind of Lippmann-Schwinger equation

\textit{\bm{\mathrm{E}}}_{f}(\mathrm{x})+\nabla\int_{\mathbf{\Omega}}\nabla\mathbcal{G}^{(k)}(x,y)\cdot\mathbcal{A}_{B}\cdot\textit{\bm{\mathrm{E}}}_{f}(y)dy-k^{2}\int_{\mathbf{\Omega}}\mathbcal{G}^{(k)}(x,y)\cdot\mathbcal{A}_{B}\cdot\textit{\bm{\mathrm{E}}}_{f}(y)dy=\mathrm{E}^{\textit{in}}(x).

(3.80)

Next, using the fact that

\bm{\Omega}=\Omega_{i}\cup\Big(\bigcup_{j\neq i}^{[d^{-3}]}\Omega_{j}\Big)\cup\Big(\bm{\Omega}\setminus\bigcup_{j=1}^{[d^{-3}]}\Omega_{j}\Big)\;\text{and}\;\textit{Vol}(\Omega_{j})=d^{3},

we express equation (
3.80
) in its discretized form at
x=z_{i}
as:

\displaystyle\textit{\bm{\mathrm{E}}}_{f}(z_{i})

\displaystyle+\nabla\int_{\Omega_{i}}\nabla\mathbcal{G}^{(0)}(z_{i},y)\cdot\mathcal{A}_{B}\cdot\textit{\bm{\mathrm{E}}}_{f}(y)\ dy-\delta^{3-h}\sum_{j\neq i}^{[d^{-3}]}\mathbcal{A}_{B}\cdot\bm{\Upsilon}^{(k)}(z_{i},z_{j})\cdot\textit{\bm{\mathrm{E}}}_{f}(z_{j})=\mathrm{E}^{\textit{in}}(z_{i})

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbf{\Omega}\setminus\bigcup\limits_{j=1}^{[d^{-3}]}\Omega_{j}}\bm{\Upsilon}^{(k)}(z_{i},y)\cdot\mathbcal{A}_{B}\cdot\textit{\bm{\mathrm{E}}}_{f}(y)-\nabla\int_{\Omega_{i}}\nabla\mathbcal{G}^{(0)}(z_{i},y)\cdot\mathcal{A}_{B}\cdot\big(\textit{\bm{\mathrm{E}}}_{f}(y)-\textit{\bm{\mathrm{E}}}_{f}(z_{i})\big)dy

\displaystyle-\sum_{j\neq i}^{[d^{-3}]}\int_{\Omega_{j}}\Big(\bm{\Upsilon}^{(k)}(z_{i},z_{j})\cdot\mathcal{A}_{B}\cdot\textit{\bm{\mathrm{E}}}_{f}(z_{j})-\bm{\Upsilon}^{(k)}(z_{i},y)\cdot\mathcal{A}_{B}\cdot\textit{\bm{\mathrm{E}}}_{f}(y)\Big)dy

\displaystyle+\int_{\Omega_{i}}\Big(\bm{\Upsilon}^{(k)}(z_{i},y)-\bm{\Upsilon}^{(0)}(z_{i},y)\Big)\cdot\mathcal{A}_{B}\cdot\textit{\bm{\mathrm{E}}}_{f}(y)dy,

(3.81)

which we rewrite as follows

\displaystyle\widehat{\textit{\bm{\mathrm{E}}}}_{f}(z_{i})-\delta^{3-h}\sum_{j\neq i}^{[d^{-3}]}\bm{\Upsilon}^{(k)}(z_{i},z_{j})\cdot\mathbcal{P}_{B}\cdot\widehat{\textit{\bm{\mathrm{E}}}}_{f}(z_{j})=\mathrm{E}^{\textit{in}}(z_{i})+\underbrace{\int_{\mathbf{\Omega}\setminus\bigcup\limits_{j=1}^{[d^{-3}]}\Omega_{j}}\bm{\Upsilon}^{(k)}(z_{i},y)\cdot\mathbcal{P}_{B}\cdot\widehat{\textit{\bm{\mathrm{E}}}}_{f}(y)}_{:=\textit{Err}^{(1)}_{i}}

\displaystyle-\underbrace{\nabla\int_{\Omega_{i}}\nabla\mathbcal{G}^{(0)}(z_{i},y)\cdot\mathbcal{P}_{B}\cdot\big(\widehat{\textit{\bm{\mathrm{E}}}}_{f}(y)-\widehat{\textit{\bm{\mathrm{E}}}}_{f}(z_{i})\big)dy}_{:=\textit{Err}^{(2)}_{i}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\sum_{j\neq i}^{[d^{-3}]}\int_{\Omega_{j}}\Big(\bm{\Upsilon}^{(k)}(z_{i},z_{j})\cdot\mathbcal{P}_{B}\cdot\widehat{\textit{\bm{\mathrm{E}}}}_{f}(z_{j})-\bm{\Upsilon}^{(k)}(z_{i},y)\cdot\mathbcal{P}_{B}\cdot\widehat{\textit{\bm{\mathrm{E}}}}_{f}(y)\Big)dy}_{:=\textit{Err}^{(3)}_{i}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\int_{\Omega_{i}}\Big(\bm{\Upsilon}^{(k)}(z_{i},y)-\bm{\Upsilon}^{(0)}(z_{i},y)\Big)\cdot\mathbcal{P}_{B}\cdot\widehat{\textit{\bm{\mathrm{E}}}}_{f}(y)dy}_{\textit{Err}^{(4)}_{i}},

(3.82)

where we impose
\displaystyle\mathbcal{P}_{B}:=\Big(\mathbb{I}+\mathbcal{A}_{B}\cdot\nabla\int_{\Omega_{i}}\nabla\mathbcal{G}^{(0)}(z_{i},y)dy\Big)^{-1}\cdot\mathbcal{A}_{B}
, and we set
\widehat{\textit{\bm{\mathrm{E}}}}_{f}:=\mathbcal{P}_{B}^{-1}\cdot\mathbcal{A}_{B}\cdot\textit{\bm{\mathrm{E}}}_{f}.
Therefore, we have

\mathbcal{A}_{B}=\Big(\mathbb{I}-\displaystyle\mathbcal{P}_{B}\cdot\nabla\int_{B}\nabla\mathbcal{G}^{(0)}(z_{i},y)dy\Big)^{-1}\cdot\displaystyle\mathbcal{P}_{B}.

(3.83)

We now proceed to estimate the fourth term of the above expression:

\displaystyle\textit{Err}^{(3)}_{i}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{j\neq i}^{[d^{-3}]}\int_{\Omega_{j}}\Big(\bm{\Upsilon}^{(k)}(z_{i},z_{j})\cdot\mathbcal{P}_{B}\cdot\widehat{\textit{\bm{\mathrm{E}}}}_{f}(z_{j})-\bm{\Upsilon}^{(k)}(z_{i},y)\cdot\mathbcal{P}_{B}\cdot\widehat{\textit{\bm{\mathrm{E}}}}_{f}(y)\Big)dy

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j\neq i}^{[d^{-3}]}\int_{\Omega_{i}}\bm{\Upsilon}^{(k)}(z_{i},z_{j})\cdot\mathbcal{A}_{B}\cdot\big(\textit{\bm{\mathrm{E}}}_{f}(z_{j})-\textit{\bm{\mathrm{E}}}_{f}(y)\big)+\sum_{j\neq i}^{[d^{-3}]}\int_{\Omega_{i}}\Big(\bm{\Upsilon}^{(k)}(z_{i},z_{j})-\bm{\Upsilon}^{(k)}(z_{i},y)\Big)\cdot\mathbcal{A}_{B}\cdot\textit{\bm{\mathrm{E}}}_{f}(y)

\displaystyle\leq\underbrace{\sum_{j\neq i}^{[d^{-3}]}\bm{\Upsilon}^{(k)}(z_{i},z_{j})\cdot\mathbcal{A}_{B}\cdot\int_{\Omega_{i}}|z_{j}-y|^{\alpha}\big[\textit{\bm{\mathrm{E}}}_{f}\big]_{C^{0,\alpha}(\overline{\bm{\Omega}})}}_{:=\textit{Err}_{\textit{ef,i,1}}}+\underbrace{\sum_{j\neq i}^{[d^{-3}]}\int_{\Omega_{j}}\Big(\bm{\Upsilon}^{(k)}(z_{i},z_{j})-\bm{\Upsilon}^{(k)}(z_{i},y)\Big)\cdot\mathbcal{A}_{B}\cdot\textit{\bm{\mathrm{E}}}_{f}(y)}_{:=\textit{Err}_{\textit{ef,i,2}}}.

By applying Lemma
3.5
and leveraging the result from
[
9
, Section 5.1]
, along with the
C^{0,\alpha}
-regularity of
\bm{E}_{f}
for
0<\alpha<1
, we can estimate the first error term as follows

\displaystyle|\textit{Err}_{\textit{ef,i,1}}|

\displaystyle=|\mathbcal{A}_{B}|\big[\textit{\bm{\mathrm{E}}}_{f}\big]_{C^{0,\alpha}(\overline{\bm{\Omega}})}\textit{Vol}(\Omega_{j})d^{\alpha}\Big|\sum_{j\neq i}^{[d^{-3}]}\bm{\Upsilon}^{(k)}(z_{i},z_{j})\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim|\mathbcal{A}_{B}|\big[\textit{\bm{\mathrm{E}}}_{f}\big]_{C^{0,\alpha}(\overline{\bm{\Omega}})}\textit{Vol}(\Omega_{j})d^{\alpha}d^{-3}|\log(d)|.

Taking the square of this expression and summing over
i=1\ \text{to}\ [d^{-3}]
yields the final estimation

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{[d^{-3}]}|\textit{Err}_{\textit{ef,i,1}}|^{2}\lesssim|\mathbcal{A}_{B}|^{2}\big[\textit{\bm{\mathrm{E}}}_{f}\big]^{2}_{C^{0,\alpha}(\overline{\bm{\Omega}})}d^{2\alpha-3}|\log(d)|^{2}.

(3.84)

Next, we proceed as follows

\displaystyle|\textit{Err}_{\textit{ef,i,2}}|

\displaystyle:=\Big|\sum_{j\neq i}^{[d^{-3}]}\int_{\Omega_{i}}\Big(\bm{\Upsilon}^{(k)}(z_{i},z_{j})-\bm{\Upsilon}^{(k)}(z_{i},y)\Big)\cdot\mathbcal{A}_{B}\cdot\textit{\bm{\mathrm{E}}}_{f}(y)\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\textit{\bm{\mathrm{E}}}_{f}\|_{L^{\infty}(\bm{\Omega})}|\mathbcal{A}_{B}|\sum_{j\neq i}^{[d^{-3}]}\int_{\Omega_{i}}\Big|\int_{0}^{1}\underset{y}{\nabla}\bm{\Upsilon}^{(k)}(z_{i},z_{j}+\theta(y-z_{j}))\cdot(y-z_{j})d\theta\Big|dy

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\textit{\bm{\mathrm{E}}}_{f}\|_{L^{\infty}(\bm{\Omega})}|\mathbcal{A}_{B}|\textit{Vol}(\Omega_{j})d\sum_{j\neq i}^{[d^{-3}]}d_{ij}^{-4}

\displaystyle=\|\textit{\bm{\mathrm{E}}}_{f}\|_{L^{\infty}(\bm{\Omega})}|\mathbcal{A}_{B}|\textit{Vol}(\Omega_{j})d\Bigg(\sum_{j\neq i}^{\textit{A}_{1}}d_{ij}^{-4}+\sum_{j\neq i}^{\textit{A}_{2}}d_{ij}^{-4}\Bigg),

where,
\textit{A}_{1}
represents the numbers of subdomains
\Omega_{j}
such that
\Omega_{j}\cap\mathcal{B}^{0}_{j}\neq{\varphi}
, with
\mathcal{B}^{0}_{j}
being a small neighborhood of
\mathcal{B}_{j}
, for which there exist
r\in]0,1[
such that

\displaystyle\textit{Vol}(\mathcal{B}^{0}_{j})=\mathcal{O}(d^{3r}),\;\text{and}\;\textit{diam}(\mathcal{B}^{0}_{j})=\mathcal{O}(d^{r}),

(3.85)

and we consider
\textit{A}_{1}
is the number of sub-domains
\Omega_{j}
such that
\Omega_{j}\cap\mathcal{B}^{0}_{j}={\varphi}.
Furthermore, the number of subdomains
\textit{A}_{1}
satisfies
\textit{A}_{1}=\mathcal{O}(d^{3r-3}),
as shown in
[
9
, Section 4]
. Squaring the above expression and summing over
i=1\ \text{to}\ [d^{-3}]
yields

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{[d^{-3}]}|\textit{Err}_{\textit{ef,i,2}}|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\textit{\bm{\mathrm{E}}}_{f}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}(\bm{\Omega})}|\mathbcal{A}_{B}|^{2}\textit{Vol}(\Omega_{j})^{2}d^{2}\Big(\textit{A}_{1}\sum_{j\neq i}^{\textit{A}_{1}}\sum_{j\neq i}^{[d^{-3}]}d_{ij}^{-8}+d^{-8r-3}\Big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\textit{\bm{\mathrm{E}}}_{f}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}(\bm{\Omega})}|\mathbcal{A}_{B}|^{2}\textit{Vol}(\Omega_{j})^{2}d^{2}\Big(\textit{A}_{1}^{2}d^{-8}+d^{-8r-3}\Big)

(3.86)

Now, choosing
r=\frac{11}{14}
results in

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{[d^{-3}]}|\textit{Err}_{\textit{ef,i,2}}|^{2}\lesssim\|\textit{\bm{\mathrm{E}}}_{f}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}(\bm{\Omega})}|\mathbcal{A}_{B}|^{2}d^{-\frac{9}{7}}.

(3.87)

Subsequently, combining the estimates (
3.84
) and (
3.87
), we arrive at

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{[d^{-3}]}|\textit{Err}_{i}^{(3)}|^{2}\lesssim\|\textit{\bm{\mathrm{E}}}_{f}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}(\bm{\Omega})}|\mathbcal{A}_{B}|^{2}d^{-\frac{9}{7}}+|\mathbcal{A}_{B}|^{2}\big[\textit{\bm{\mathrm{E}}}_{f}\big]^{2}_{C^{0,\alpha}(\overline{\bm{\Omega}})}d^{2\alpha-3}|\log(d)|^{2}.

(3.88)

To estimate the term
\textit{Err}^{(1)}_{i}
, we utilize the following counting lemma, as described in
[
9
, Lemma 6.2]
.

Lemma 3.4
.

For any fixed
z_{i},i=1,2,\ldots,[d^{-3}]
, the following counting estimate holds

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{[d^{-3}]}\Big|\int_{\mathbf{\Omega}\setminus\bigcup\limits_{j=1}^{[d^{-3}]}\Omega_{j}}\bm{\Upsilon}^{(k)}(z_{i},y)dy\Big|^{2}=\mathcal{O}(d^{-1}).

(3.89)

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle|\textit{Err}^{(1)}_{i}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\Big|\int_{\mathbf{\Omega}\setminus\bigcup\limits_{j=1}^{[d^{-3}]}\Omega_{j}}\bm{\Upsilon}^{(k)}(z_{i},y)\cdot\mathbcal{P}_{B}\cdot\widehat{\textit{\bm{\mathrm{E}}}}_{f}(y)\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\textit{\bm{\mathrm{E}}}_{f}\|_{L^{\infty}(\bm{\Omega})}|\mathbcal{A}_{B}|\int_{\mathbf{\Omega}\setminus\bigcup\limits_{j=1}^{[d^{-3}]}\Omega_{j}}\bm{\Upsilon}^{(k)}(z_{i},y)dy.

Consequently, taking square, summing up to
[d^{-3}]
, and using the previous lemma, we deduce

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{[d^{-3}]}|\textit{Err}^{(1)}_{i}|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\textit{\bm{\mathrm{E}}}_{f}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}(\bm{\Omega})}|\mathbcal{A}_{B}|^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{[d^{-3}]}\Big|\int_{\mathbf{\Omega}\setminus\bigcup\limits_{j=1}^{[d^{-3}]}\Omega_{j}}\bm{\Upsilon}^{(k)}(z_{i},y)dy\Big|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\textit{\bm{\mathrm{E}}}_{f}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}(\bm{\Omega})}|\mathbcal{A}_{B}|^{2}d^{-1}.

(3.90)

We now recall that

\displaystyle|\textit{Err}^{(4)}_{i}|

\displaystyle:=\Bigg|\int_{\Omega_{i}}\Big(\bm{\Upsilon}^{(k)}(z_{i},y)-\bm{\Upsilon}^{(0)}(z_{i},y)\Big)\cdot\mathcal{P}_{B}\cdot\hat{\textit{\bm{\mathrm{E}}}}_{f}(y)dy\Bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\textit{\bm{\mathrm{E}}}_{f}\|_{L^{\infty}(\overline{\bm{\Omega}})}|\mathbcal{A}_{B}|\int_{D_{j}}\frac{1}{|z_{i}-y|}\ dy

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\textit{\bm{\mathrm{E}}}_{f}\|_{L^{\infty}(\overline{\bm{\Omega}})}|\mathbcal{A}_{B}|\ d^{2},

(3.91)

which further implies after taking the square modulus and summing from
1
to
[d^{-3}]
as

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{[d^{-3}]}|\textit{Err}^{(5)}_{i}|^{2}\lesssim|\mathbcal{A}_{B}|^{2}\ \|\textit{\bm{\mathrm{E}}}_{f}\|_{L^{\infty}(\overline{\bm{\Omega}})}\ d.

(3.92)

Next, in order to estimate the following term, we use
C^{0,\alpha}
-regularity of
\bm{E}_{f}
for
0<\alpha<1
. Consequently, we derive

\displaystyle|\textit{Err}^{(2)}_{i}|

\displaystyle:=\Big|\nabla\int_{\Omega_{i}}\nabla\mathbcal{G}^{(0)}(z_{i},y)\cdot\mathcal{P}_{B}\cdot\big(\widehat{\textit{\bm{\mathrm{E}}}}_{f}(y)-\widehat{\textit{\bm{\mathrm{E}}}}_{f}(z_{i})\big)dy\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim|\mathcal{A}_{B}|\big[\textit{\bm{\mathrm{E}}}_{f}\big]_{C^{0,\alpha}}\int_{\Omega_{i}}\frac{1}{|z_{i}-y|^{3-\alpha}}dy,

(3.93)

and by a scaling we derive the following estimate

\displaystyle|\textit{Err}^{(2)}_{i}|\lesssim|\mathcal{A}_{B}|\big[\textit{\bm{\mathrm{E}}}_{f}\big]_{C^{0,\alpha}}\textit{Vol}(\Omega_{i})d^{\alpha-3}.

(3.94)

Similar to the previous estimates, we square the above expression and summing from
1
to
[d^{-3}]
, we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{[d^{-3}]}|\textit{Err}^{(2)}_{i}|^{2}\lesssim|\mathbcal{A}_{B}|^{2}\big[\textit{\bm{\mathrm{E}}}_{f}\big]^{2}_{C^{0,\alpha}(\overline{\bm{\Omega}})}d^{2\alpha-3}.

(3.95)

Therefore, we obtain the following linear algebraic system

\displaystyle\Big(\widehat{\textit{\bm{\mathrm{E}}}}_{f}(z_{i})-\tilde{\mathbcal{Q}}_{i}\Big)

\displaystyle-\eta\sum_{j\neq i}^{M}\bm{\Upsilon}^{(k)}(z_{i},z_{j})\cdot\mathbcal{P}_{D_{j}}\cdot\Big(\widehat{\textit{\bm{\mathrm{E}}}}_{f}(z_{i})-\tilde{\mathbcal{Q}}_{j}\Big)=\mathcal{R}_{i},\;\texttt{for}\ j=1,2,\ldots,M,

where, based on the estimates (
3.84
), (
3.87
), (
3.90
), (
3.92
) and (
3.94
), we estimated the term
\mathcal{R}_{i}
as

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{[d^{-3}]}|\mathcal{R}_{i}|^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\Big(|\mathbcal{A}_{B}|^{2}\big[\textit{\bm{\mathrm{E}}}_{f}\big]^{2}_{C^{0,\alpha}(\overline{\bm{\Omega}})}d^{2\alpha-3}|\log(d)|^{2}+|\mathbcal{A}_{B}|^{2}\big[\textit{\bm{\mathrm{E}}}_{f}\big]^{2}_{C^{0,\alpha}(\overline{\bm{\Omega}})}d^{2\alpha-3}+\|\textit{\bm{\mathrm{E}}}_{f}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}(\bm{\Omega})}|\mathbcal{A}_{B}|^{2}d^{-\frac{9}{7}}

\displaystyle+\|\textit{\bm{\mathrm{E}}}_{f}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}(\bm{\Omega})}|\mathbcal{A}_{B}|^{2}d^{-1}+|\mathbcal{A}_{B}|^{2}\ \|\textit{\bm{\mathrm{E}}}_{f}\|_{L^{\infty}(\overline{\bm{\Omega}})}\ d\Big)

(3.96)

In conclusion, due to the invertibility condition of the linear algebric system (
3.77
) and by the previous estimate, we deduce that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{[d^{-3}]}\|\widehat{\textit{\bm{\mathrm{E}}}}_{f}(z_{i})-\tilde{\mathbcal{Q}}_{i}\|_{\ell}^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\Big(|\mathbcal{A}_{B}|^{2}\big[\textit{\bm{\mathrm{E}}}_{f}\big]^{2}_{C^{0,\alpha}(\overline{\bm{\Omega}})}d^{2\alpha-3}|\log(d)|^{2}+|\mathbcal{A}_{B}|^{2}\big[\textit{\bm{\mathrm{E}}}_{f}\big]^{2}_{C^{0,\alpha}(\overline{\bm{\Omega}})}d^{2\alpha-3}+\|\textit{\bm{\mathrm{E}}}_{f}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}(\bm{\Omega})}|\mathbcal{A}_{B}|^{2}d^{-\frac{9}{7}}

\displaystyle+\|\textit{\bm{\mathrm{E}}}_{f}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}(\bm{\Omega})}|\mathbcal{P}_{B}|^{2}d^{-1}+|\mathbcal{A}_{B}|^{2}\ \|\textit{\bm{\mathrm{E}}}_{f}\|_{L^{\infty}(\overline{\bm{\Omega}})}\delta^{4}\ d^{-3}\Big).

(3.97)

As, each term of the above expression is square summable, based on the above estimate, we therefore obtain that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{[d^{-3}]}\Big\|\big|\widehat{\textit{\bm{\mathrm{E}}}}_{f}(z_{i})\big|^{2}-\big|\tilde{\mathbcal{Q}}_{i}\big|^{2}\Big\|_{\ell^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup\limits_{B}\Big|\widehat{\textit{\bm{\mathrm{E}}}}_{f}(z_{i})+\tilde{\mathbcal{Q}}_{i}\Big|^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{[d^{-3}]}\|\widehat{\textit{\bm{\mathrm{E}}}}_{f}(z_{i})-\tilde{\mathbcal{Q}}_{i}\|_{\ell^{2}}^{2}\lesssim d^{-\frac{9}{7}}.

(3.98)

3.3
End of the Proof of Theorem
1.2
: The Asymptotic Expansions

This section begins by estimating the term
\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=1\end{subarray}}^{M}|\sigma^{(i)}(t)-\bm{\mathrm{Y}}(z_{i},t)|^{2}
, where
(\sigma^{(i)})_{i=1}^{M}
represents the vector-valued solution to the Volterra-type system of integral equations (
3.2
) and
\bm{\mathrm{Y}}(z_{i},t)
,
i=1,\ldots,M
, denotes the corresponding solution of the Lippmann-Schwinger equation (
3.1
) with zero initial conditions. Next, using the fact that

\bm{\Omega}=\Omega_{i}\cup\Big(\bigcup_{j\neq i}^{[d^{-3}]}\Omega_{j}\Big)\cup\Big(\bm{\Omega}\setminus\bigcup_{j=1}^{[d^{-3}]}\Omega_{j}\Big)\;\text{and}\;\textit{Vol}(\Omega_{j})=d^{3},

we rewrite the expression (
3.1
) in a discretized form at
\mathrm{x}=\mathrm{z}_{i}

\displaystyle\bm{\bm{\mathrm{Y}}}(\mathrm{z}_{i},\mathrm{t})+\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}j=1\\
j\neq i\end{subarray}}^{M}\overline{b}\ \delta^{3-\beta}\int_{0}^{t}\Phi^{(m)}(z_{i},t;z_{j},\tau)\ \frac{\partial}{\partial\tau}\bm{\mathrm{Y}}(z_{j},\tau)\ d\tau=\mathbcal{F}(z_{i},t)+E_{(\mathbf{1})}+E_{(\mathbf{2})}+E_{(\mathbf{3})},

(3.99)

or, equivalently,

\displaystyle\bm{\bm{\mathrm{Y}}}(\mathrm{z}_{i},\mathrm{t})+\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}j=1\\
j\neq i\end{subarray}}^{M}\overline{b}\ \delta^{3-\beta}\int_{0}^{t}\Phi^{(m)}(z_{i},t;z_{j},\tau)\ \frac{\partial}{\partial\tau}\bm{\mathrm{Y}}(z_{j},\tau)\ d\tau

\displaystyle=\overline{a}\,f(t)\,\delta^{\beta-h}\,\mathbcal{P}_{B}\cdot\widehat{\bm{E}}_{f}(z_{i})\cdot\overline{\widehat{\bm{E}}}^{\textit{Tr}}_{f}(z_{i})

\displaystyle+E_{(\mathbf{1})}+E_{(\mathbf{2})}+E_{(\mathbf{3})},

(3.100)

where, we set
\widehat{\bm{E}}_{f}:=\mathbcal{P}_{B}^{-1}\cdot\mathbcal{A}_{B}\cdot\bm{E}_{f}
,

E_{(\mathbf{1})}:=-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbf{\Omega}\setminus\bigcup\limits_{j=1}^{[d^{-3}]}\Omega_{j}}\overline{b}\ \Phi^{(m)}(z_{i},t;y,\tau)\ \frac{\partial}{\partial\tau}\bm{\mathrm{Y}}(y,\tau)\ dyd\tau,

E_{(\mathbf{2})}:=-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega_{j}}\overline{b}\ \Phi^{(m)}(z_{i},t;y,\tau)\ \frac{\partial}{\partial\tau}\bm{\mathrm{Y}}(y,\tau)\ dyd\tau,

and

E_{(\mathbf{3})}:=-\int_{0}^{t}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}j=1\\
j\neq i\end{subarray}}^{M}\int_{\Omega_{i}}\overline{b}\ \Phi^{(m)}(z_{i},t;y,\tau)\ \frac{\partial}{\partial\tau}\bm{\mathrm{Y}}(y,\tau)\ dyd\tau+\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}j=1\\
j\neq i\end{subarray}}^{M}\overline{b}\ \delta^{3-\beta}\int_{0}^{t}\Phi^{(m)}(z_{i},t;z_{j},\tau)\ \frac{\partial}{\partial\tau}\bm{\mathrm{Y}}(z_{j},\tau)\ d\tau.

Before estimating the terms introduced earlier, we first recall a useful result, stated as follows:

Lemma 3.5
.

[
1
]

Counting Lemma.

For any arbitrary distribution of points
z_{j},j=1,\ldots,M
, within a bounded domain of
\mathbb{R}^{3}
(with a prescribed minimum distance
d
between any two points), the following estimates hold uniformly with respect to
i
:

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}j=1\\
j\neq i\end{subarray}}^{M}\frac{1}{|z_{i}-z_{j}|^{k}}=\begin{cases}\mathcal{O}(d^{-3})\;&\text{if}\;k<3,\\
\mathcal{O}\Big(d^{-3}(1+|\log(d)|)\Big)\;&\text{if}\;k=3,\\
\mathcal{O}(d^{-k})\;&\text{if}\;k>3.\end{cases}

To estimate the term
E_{(\mathbf{1})}
, we adopt the framework and notations introduced in
[
33
,
34
]
. The analysis proceeds by addressing the following two scenarios:

\mathbf{\Omega}
\aleph_{(1)}\ (\text{The region above the blue line})
\aleph_{(2)}(\text{The region below the blue line})
\mathrm{D}_{i}
z_{i}
\bullet

Figure 3.1:
A schematic representation of the partitioning of the region
\mathbf{\Omega}\setminus\bigcup\limits_{j=1}^{[d^{-3}]}\Omega_{j}
.

1.

Case 1:
z_{i}
Far from the Boundary
\partial\mathbf{\Omega}

.

When the point
z_{i}
is sufficiently distant from the boundary
\partial\mathbf{\Omega}
, the function
|z_{i}-z|^{-1}
remains bounded in the vicinity of the boundary. Consequently, in this scenario, the error term
E_{(\mathbf{1})}
scales as

E_{(\mathbf{1})}=\mathcal{O}\left(\text{vol}\left(\mathbf{\Omega}\setminus\bigcup_{j=1}^{\lfloor d^{-3}\rfloor}\Omega_{j}\right)\right)=\mathcal{O}(d),

where the volume of the excluded region is directly proportional to
d
, assuming
d\ll 1
.

2.

Case 2:
z_{i}
Near the Boundary
\partial\mathbf{\Omega}
.

When
z_{i}
approaches the boundary
\partial\mathbf{\Omega}
or is near one of the sub-regions
\Omega_{j}
, the estimation is divided into two components. Let the contribution from the
\Omega_{j}
-regions near
z_{i}
be denoted as
\aleph_{(1)}
, and the contribution from the remainder of the domain as
\aleph_{(2)}
.

(a)

Contribution from
\aleph_{(2)}
:

The integral over
\aleph_{(2)}
is evaluated similarly to the previous case. Since
\aleph_{(2)}\subset\mathbf{\Omega}\setminus\bigcup\limits_{j=1}^{\lfloor d^{-3}\rfloor}\Omega_{j}
, the volume of
\aleph_{(2)}
, denoted as
\text{vol}(\aleph_{(2)})
, scales as
d
for
d\ll 1
.

(b)

Contribution from
\aleph_{(1)}
:

To estimate the integral over
\aleph_{(1)}
, we first determine the number of
\Omega_{j}
-regions near
z_{i}
. These regions are localized near a small segment of the boundary
\partial\mathbf{\Omega}
. Assuming
\partial\mathbf{\Omega}
is sufficiently smooth, this segment can be approximated as flat and centered around
z_{i}
. This flat region is partitioned into concentric square layers centered at
z_{i}
, as illustrated in Figure
3.1
.

Given that the characteristic size of the flat region is of order unity relative to the parameter
d\ll 1
, and that the maximum edge length of the squares (or subregions
\Omega_{j}
) is
d
, the number of layers is at most
\lfloor d^{-1}\rfloor
. In the
n^{\text{th}}
layer (
n=0,\ldots,\lfloor d^{-1}\rfloor
), the number of squares is at most
(2n+1)^{2}
.
Excluding the innermost layer (
n=0
), the number of squares in the
n^{\text{th}}
layer is bounded by

(2n+1)^{2}-(2n-1)^{2},

and their minimum distance from
z_{i}
is approximately
n\left(d-\frac{d^{3}}{2}\right)
.

This framework provides a systematic approach for estimating the integral contributions from regions near and far from the boundary.

Therefore, we express the following term and, using the singularity estimate with
\beta>\frac{1}{2}
, we obtain

\int_{0}^{t}|\Phi^{(m)}(x,t;z,\tau)|\ d\tau=\mathcal{O}(|x-z|^{-1}),

where
\Phi^{(m)}(x,t;z,\tau)
denotes the fundamental solution with the corresponding singular behavior. We have

\displaystyle|E_{(\mathbf{1})}|

\displaystyle=\Bigg|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbf{\Omega}\setminus\bigcup\limits_{j=1}^{[d^{-3}]}\Omega_{j}}\overline{b}\ \Phi^{(m)}(z_{i},t;y,\tau)\ \frac{\partial}{\partial\tau}\bm{\mathrm{Y}}(y,\tau)\ dyd\tau\Bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\Bigg|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\aleph_{(1)}}\overline{b}\ \Phi^{(m)}(z_{i},t;y,\tau)\ \frac{\partial}{\partial\tau}\bm{\mathrm{Y}}(y,\tau)\ dyd\tau\Bigg|+\Bigg|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\aleph_{(2)}}\overline{b}\ \Phi^{(m)}(z_{i},t;y,\tau)\ \frac{\partial}{\partial\tau}\bm{\mathrm{Y}}(y,\tau)\ dyd\tau\Bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}j=1\end{subarray}}^{[d^{-1}]}\frac{1}{d_{ij}}\|\frac{\partial}{\partial t}{\bm{\mathrm{Y}}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}\big(0,\mathrm{T};\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\mathbf{\Omega})\big)}}\textit{vol}\big(\Omega_{j}\big)+\|\frac{\partial}{\partial t}{\bm{\mathrm{Y}}\|_{\mathrm{C}^{1}\big(0,\mathrm{T};\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\mathbf{\Omega})\big)}}\|\Phi^{(m)}(z_{i},t;y,\tau)\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}\big(0,\mathrm{T};\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\mathbf{A}_{(2)})\big)}\textit{vol}\big(\aleph_{(2)}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim d^{3}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}j=1\end{subarray}}^{[d^{-1}]}\frac{1}{d_{ij}}+d\lesssim d^{3}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}n=1\end{subarray}}^{[d^{-1}]}\big[(2n+1)^{2}-(2n-1)^{2}\big]\frac{1}{n(d-\frac{d^{3}}{2})}+d\lesssim d^{3}\cdot d^{-2}+d.

Hence, we obtain

\displaystyle|E_{(\mathbf{1})}|=\mathcal{O}\big(d\big).

(3.101)

Next, Using Lemma
3.2
and Corollary
3.1
, we have
\frac{\partial}{\partial t}{\bm{\mathrm{Y}}}\in\mathrm{L}^{\infty}\big(0,\mathrm{T};\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\mathbf{\Omega})\big)
and due to the singularity estimates introduced in (
3.15
), we deduce that

\displaystyle|E_{(\mathbf{2})}|

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\Big(\overline{b}\;\|\frac{\partial}{\partial t}{\bm{\mathrm{Y}}}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}\big(0,\mathrm{T};\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\mathbf{\Omega})\big)}\int_{\Omega_{i}}|y-z_{i}|^{2r-3}\;dy\Big).

To analyze the term
\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{i}}|y-z_{i}|^{2r-3}\;dy
, for
0<r<1
, we conclude that
\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{i}}|y-z_{i}|^{2r-3}\;dy=\mathcal{O}(\delta^{\frac{2r}{3}}).
Therefore, we deduce that

\displaystyle|E_{(\mathbf{2})}|=\mathcal{O}\Big(d^{2r}\Big).

(3.102)

We now proceed to estimate the third term,
E_{(\mathbf{3})}
. Specifically, we have

\displaystyle E_{(\mathbf{3})}

\displaystyle:=-\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}j=1\\
j\neq i\end{subarray}}^{[d^{-3}]}\overline{b}\int_{\Omega_{j}}\Bigg[\int_{0}^{t}\Phi^{(m)}(z_{i},t;y,\tau)\ \frac{\partial}{\partial\tau}\bm{\mathrm{Y}}(y,\tau)\ dyd\tau-\int_{0}^{t}\Phi^{(m)}(z_{i},t;z_{j},\tau)\ \frac{\partial}{\partial\tau}\bm{\mathrm{Y}}(z_{j},\tau)\ d\tau\Bigg]\;dyd\tau.

\displaystyle=-\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}j=1\\
j\neq i\end{subarray}}^{[d^{-3}]}\overline{b}\int_{\Omega_{j}}\Bigg[\underbrace{\frac{\partial}{\partial\tau}\bm{\mathrm{Y}}(y,\tau)\int_{0}^{t}\Big(\Phi^{(m)}(z_{i},t;y,\tau)-\Phi^{(m)}(z_{i},t;z_{j},\tau)\Big)\;dyd\tau.}_{\textbf{err}_{(1)}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\int_{0}^{t}\Phi^{(m)}(z_{i},t;z_{j},\tau)\Big[\frac{\partial}{\partial t}{\bm{\mathrm{Y}}(y,\tau)}-\frac{\partial}{\partial t}{\bm{\mathrm{Y}}(z_{j},\tau)}\Big]}_{\textbf{err}_{(2)}}\Bigg]\;dyd\tau.

(3.103)

Using the singularity estimate of the heat fundamental solution, as outlined in
[
12
, Chapter 1]
and
[
17
, Chapter 9]
, for
0<\beta<1
, we obtain

\int_{0}^{t}|\nabla_{y}\Phi^{(m)}(z_{i},t;z_{j},\tau)|\ d\tau=\mathcal{O}(|x-z|^{2r-4}),

we estimate
\text{err}_{(1)}
as

\displaystyle\textbf{err}_{(1)}:=\Bigg|

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}j=1\\
j\neq i\end{subarray}}^{[d^{-3}]}\overline{b}\int_{\Omega_{j}}\frac{\partial}{\partial\tau}\bm{\mathrm{Y}}(y,\tau)\int_{0}^{t}\Big(\Phi^{(m)}(z_{i},t;z_{j},\tau)-\Phi^{(m)}(z_{i},t;z_{j},\tau)\Big)\;dyd\tau\Bigg|

\displaystyle=\Bigg|\frac{1}{4\pi}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}j=1\\
j\neq i\end{subarray}}^{[d^{-3}]}\overline{b}\int_{\Omega_{j}}\frac{\partial}{\partial\tau}\bm{\mathrm{Y}}(y,\tau)\ (y-z_{j})\int_{0}^{t}\nabla_{y}\Phi^{(m)}(z_{i},t;z^{*}_{j},\tau)\ dyd\tau\Bigg|,\quad\text{with}\;z_{j}^{*}\in\Omega_{j}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\Bigg(\overline{b}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}j=1\\
j\neq i\end{subarray}}^{[d^{-3}]}\frac{1}{d^{-4+2r}_{ij}}\;\|\frac{\partial}{\partial t}{\bm{\mathrm{Y}}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}\big(0,\mathrm{T};\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\mathbf{\Omega})\big)}}\;\int_{\Omega_{j}}|y-z_{j}|dy\Bigg)=\mathcal{O}\Bigg(d^{4}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}j=1\\
j\neq i\end{subarray}}^{[d^{-3}]}\frac{1}{d^{-4+2r}_{ij}}\Bigg).

(3.104)

Next, we deduce using Taylor’s series expansion that

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial t}{\bm{\mathrm{Y}}(y,\tau)}-\frac{\partial}{\partial t}{\bm{\mathrm{Y}}(z_{j},\tau)}=(y-z_{j})\;\nabla_{y}\frac{\partial}{\partial t}{\bm{\mathrm{Y}}(z^{*}_{j},\tau)},

(3.105)

where,
z^{*}\in\Omega_{j}.

Therefore, due to Corollary
3.1
, we have
\partial_{x_{i}}\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\bm{\mathrm{Y}}\in L^{\infty}\big(0,\mathrm{T};\mathrm{L}^{p}(\mathbf{\Omega})\big)
for
p>3
and using the singularity estimate with
0<r<1
, we obtain

\int_{0}^{t}|\Phi^{(m)}(z_{i},t;z_{j},\tau)|\ d\tau=\mathcal{O}(|x-z|^{2r-3}),

\displaystyle\textbf{err}_{(2)}

\displaystyle:=\Bigg|\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}j=1\\
j\neq i\end{subarray}}^{[d^{-3}]}\overline{b}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega_{j}}(y-z_{j})\ \Phi^{(m)}(z_{i},t;z_{j},\tau)\ \frac{\partial}{\partial_{x}}\frac{\partial}{\partial t}{\bm{\mathrm{Y}}(z_{j}^{*},\tau)}\ dyd\tau\Bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}j=1\\
j\neq i\end{subarray}}^{[d^{-3}]}\Bigg(\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega_{j}}(y-z_{j})^{q}|\Phi^{(m)}(z_{i},t;z_{j},\tau)|^{q}\ dyd\tau\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{q}}\|\partial_{x_{i}}\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\bm{\mathrm{Y}}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}\big(0,\mathrm{T};\mathrm{L}^{p}(\mathbf{\Omega})\big)},\;\text{with}\ \frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{q}=1.

Now, due to the singularity estimate of
\Phi^{(m)}(z_{i},\mathrm{t};z_{j},\tau)
, we have

|\Phi^{(m)}(z_{i},\mathrm{t};z_{j},\tau)|\lesssim\frac{1}{(t-\tau)^{qr}}\frac{1}{|z_{i}-z_{j}|^{q(3-2r)}},\;\text{with}\;z_{i}\neq z_{j}.

The above function is integrable in
\Omega_{j}\times(0,T)
only when
qr<1
. Then, by Corollary
3.1
, and choosing
p
sufficiently large, we take
q
close to 1. Considering
r<1
, we then see that

\displaystyle\textbf{err}_{(2)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}j=1\\
j\neq i\end{subarray}}^{[d^{-3}]}\frac{1}{d^{q(-3+2r)}_{ij}}\;\|\partial_{x_{i}}\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\bm{\mathrm{Y}}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}\big(0,\mathrm{T};\mathrm{L}^{p}(\mathbf{\Omega})\big)}\;\int_{\Omega_{j}}|y-z_{j}|^{q}dy\Bigg)=\mathcal{O}\Bigg(d^{3+q}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}j=1\\
j\neq i\end{subarray}}^{[d^{-3}]}\frac{1}{d^{q(-3+2r)}_{ij}}\Bigg).

(3.106)

Further, based on the estimates (
3.104
) and (
3.106
), we derive the following estimate

\displaystyle E_{(\mathbf{3})}=\mathcal{O}\Big(\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}j=1\\
j\neq i\end{subarray}}^{[d^{-3}]}\frac{1}{d^{-4+2r}_{ij}}\Big)d^{4}=\mathcal{O}\Big(d\Big).

(3.107)

Comparing the equations (
3.2
) and (
3.100
), we arrive at the following expression with
\mathbcal{B}_{i}(z_{i},t):=\bm{\bm{\mathrm{Y}}}(\mathrm{z}_{i},\mathrm{t})-\sigma^{(i)}(t)

\displaystyle\mathbcal{B}_{i}(z_{i},t)+\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}j=1\\
j\neq i\end{subarray}}^{M}\overline{b}\ \delta^{3-\beta}\int_{0}^{t}\Phi^{(m)}(z_{i},t;z_{j},\tau)\ \frac{\partial}{\partial\tau}\mathbcal{B}_{j}(z_{j},t)\ d\tau=\overline{a}f(t)\delta^{\beta-h}\ \mathbcal{P}_{B}\Big(\big|\widehat{\bm{\textit{E}}}_{f}(z_{i})\big|^{2}-\big|\tilde{\mathbcal{Q}}_{i}\big|^{2}\Big)+\mathcal{O}(d).

(3.108)

Gathering (
3.101
), (
3.102
) and (
3.107
), we get

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{[d^{-3}]}\Big(|E_{(\mathbf{1})}|^{2}+|E_{(\mathbf{2})}|^{2}+|E_{(\mathbf{3})}|^{2}\Big)=\mathcal{O}\Big(M\;d^{2}+M\;d^{4r}\Big)=\mathcal{O}(d^{-1}).

(3.109)

Thus, applying the above estimate together with the invertibility property and the estimate (
2.97
) for the algebraic system (
2.92
), we use the same method to that applied to (
3.108
) to derive the following estimate as
d\ll 1

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{M}|\sigma^{(i)}(t)-\bm{\mathrm{Y}}(z_{i},t)|^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(d^{-1})+\mathcal{O}\Bigg(\delta^{2\beta-2h}\sum_{i=1}^{[d^{-3}]}\Big\|\big|\widehat{\bm{\textit{E}}}_{f}(z_{i})\big|^{2}-\big|\tilde{\mathbcal{Q}}_{i}\big|^{2}\Big\|_{\ell^{2}}^{2}\Bigg).

(3.110)

Then, based on the estimate (
3.98
) proved in Section (
3.2.3
), we have the following estimate

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{[d^{-3}]}\Big\|\big|\widehat{\bm{\textit{E}}}_{f}(z_{i})\big|^{2}-\big|\tilde{\mathbcal{Q}}_{i}\big|^{2}\Big\|_{\ell^{2}}^{2}\lesssim d^{-\frac{9}{7}}.

(3.111)

Consequently, we derive that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{M}|\sigma^{(i)}(t)-\bm{\mathrm{Y}}(z_{i},t)|^{2}=\mathcal{O}(d^{-1})+\mathcal{O}\Big(\delta^{2\beta-2h}d^{-\frac{9}{7}}\Big).

(3.112)

We introduce the unknown variable
\bm{\mathrm{Y}}=\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\mathbf{U}
, where
\mathbf{U}
satisfies the following Lippmann-Schwinger equation

\bm{\mathrm{U}}(\mathrm{x},\mathrm{t})+\overline{b}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbf{\Omega}}\Phi^{(m)}(x,t;y,\tau)\ \frac{\partial}{\partial\tau}\bm{\mathrm{U}}(y,\tau)\ dyd\tau=\mathbcal{F}(\mathrm{x},\mathrm{t}),\mbox{ for }\mathrm{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{3},\mathrm{t}\in(0,\mathrm{T}),

with zero initial conditions for
\mathbf{U}
up to the first order and define
Let us now define

\displaystyle\mathbcal{W}(x,t):=\begin{cases}\mathbf{U}(x,t)&\text{if}\;(x,t)\in\mathbf{\Omega}\times(0,T)\\
\displaystyle\mathbcal{F}(\mathrm{x},\mathrm{t})-\overline{b}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbf{\Omega}}\Phi^{(m)}(x,t;y,\tau)\ \frac{\partial}{\partial\tau}\bm{\mathrm{U}}(y,\tau)\ dyd\tau&\text{for}\;(x,t)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\setminus\mathbf{\Omega}\times(0,T).\end{cases}

(3.113)

From this point onward, our objective is to estimate
|\mathbcal{W}^{\textit{sc}}(x,t)-u^{\textit{sc}}(x,t)|
. To proceed, we assume that the point
x
lies outside the region
\mathbf{\Omega}\cup\{x_{0}\}
. Under this assumption, we have

\displaystyle\mathbcal{W}^{\textit{sc}}(x,t)

\displaystyle=-\overline{b}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbf{\Omega}}\Phi^{(m)}(x,t;y,\tau)\ \frac{\partial}{\partial\tau}\bm{\mathrm{U}}(y,\tau)\ dyd\tau

\displaystyle=-\overline{b}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbf{\Omega}}\Phi^{(m)}(x,t;y,\tau)\ \bm{\mathrm{Y}}(y,\tau)\ dyd\tau

\displaystyle=-\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=1\end{subarray}}^{[d^{-3}]}\overline{b}\ \delta^{3-\beta}\;\int_{0}^{t}\Phi^{(m)}(x,t;z_{i},\tau)\bm{\mathrm{Y}}(z_{i},\tau)d\tau-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbf{\Omega}\setminus\bigcup\limits_{i=1}^{[d^{-3}]}\Omega_{i}}\overline{b}\ \Phi^{(m)}(x,t;z_{i},\tau)\ \frac{\partial}{\partial\tau}\bm{\mathrm{Y}}(y,\tau)\ dyd\tau

\displaystyle-\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=1\end{subarray}}^{[d^{-3}]}\overline{b}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega_{i}}\Big(\Phi^{(m)}(x,t;y,\tau)\ \bm{\mathrm{Y}}(y,\tau)-\Phi^{(m)}(x,t;z_{i},\tau)\bm{\mathrm{Y}}(z_{i},\tau)\Big)\;dyd\tau.

Using similar techniques as those employed to estimate
E_{(\mathbf{1})}
and
E_{(\mathbf{3})}
, it can be shown that the second and third terms in the above expression are of the order
\mathcal{O}(d)
as
d\ll 1
. Consequently, we deduce that

\displaystyle\mathbcal{W}^{\textit{sc}}(x,t)

\displaystyle=-\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=1\end{subarray}}^{[d^{-3}]}\overline{b}\ \delta^{3-\beta}\;\int_{0}^{t}\Phi^{(m)}(x,t;z_{i},\tau)\bm{\mathrm{Y}}(z_{i},\tau)d\tau+\mathcal{O}(d)

\displaystyle=-\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=1\end{subarray}}^{[d^{-3}]}\overline{b}\ \delta^{3-\beta}\;\int_{0}^{t}\Phi^{(m)}(x,t;z_{i},\tau)\sigma^{(i)}(\tau)d\tau+\underbrace{\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=1\end{subarray}}^{[d^{-3}]}\overline{b}\ \delta^{3-\beta}\;\int_{0}^{t}\Phi^{(m)}(x,t;z_{i},\tau)\ \big(\sigma^{(i)}-\bm{\mathrm{Y}}(z_{i},\tau)\big)(\tau)d\tau}_{:=\textbf{err}_{(3)}}

\displaystyle+\mathcal{O}(d).

(3.114)

We then apply the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality along with the estimate (
3.112
) to derive the following bound

\displaystyle\textbf{err}_{(3)}

\displaystyle:=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=1\end{subarray}}^{[d^{-3}]}\overline{b}\ \delta^{3-\beta}\;\int_{0}^{t}\Phi^{(m)}(x,t;z_{i},\tau)\ \big(\sigma^{(i)}-\bm{\mathrm{Y}}(z_{i},\tau)\big)(\tau)d\tau

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\Bigg(\delta^{3-\beta}\ \Big(\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=1\end{subarray}}^{[d^{-3}]}\int_{0}^{t}|\Phi^{(m)}(x,t;z_{i},\tau)|^{2}d\tau\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\;\Big(\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=1\end{subarray}}^{[d^{-3}]}|\sigma^{(i)}-\bm{\mathrm{Y}}(z_{i},\tau)|^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Bigg)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(\delta^{3-\beta}\ d^{-\frac{3}{2}}\ \delta^{2\beta-2h}d^{-\frac{9}{7}}).

Consequently, due to the chosen regime as mentioned in (
1.24
) i.e.

\displaystyle h=\beta\;\text{and}\;d\sim\delta^{1-\frac{\beta}{3}},

we conclude from (
3.114
) that

\displaystyle u^{\textit{sc}}(x,t)-\mathbcal{W}^{\textit{sc}}(x,t)=\mathcal{O}(\delta^{\frac{2}{7}(3-\beta)}\;\text{as}\;\delta\to 0,

which completes the proof.∎
